Brock Bowers, Brandon Jones both inactive for Raiders-Broncos

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Fernando Mendoza will not have his top pass-catcher for his second career start.

Tight end Brock Bowers is officially inactive for the Raiders’ matchup against the Broncos on Sunday.

Bowers is dealing with ankle and quad injuries. While he didn’t practice on Wednesday, he was limited on Thursday and Friday.

Bowers has 50 receptions for 676 yards with six touchdowns so far in 2026.

Receiver Dont’e Thornton, receiver Malik Benson, safety Tristin McCollum, safety Dalton Johnson, guard Bryce Cabeldue, and defensive tackle Tonka Hemingway are also inactive for Las Vegas.

For Denver, safety Brandon Jones is inactive due to a hamstring injury. Quarterback Sam Ehlinger, defensive lineman Jordan Jackson, receiver Lil’Jordan Humphrey, offensive lineman Kage Casey, tight end Dallen Bentley, and offensive lineman Gavin Ortega are also inactive for Denver.

The Beyond Devastating ‘Sunrise on the Reaping’ Ending, Explained

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Crying too hard at the end of The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping to fully grasp those final scenes? It can happen to the best of us. Let’s break down the ending of Sunrise on the Reaping to make sure you didn’t miss anything, including some of the things that Suzanne Collins’ book explains in a tiiiiiny bit more detail.

It should come as no surprise that Haymitch wins the Hunger Games. You should know that going in, even if you haven’t read Sunrise on the Reaping. When we first met the character in The Hunger Games, he was Katniss and Peeta’s mentor because he was District 12’s only living victor.

What we didn’t totally know before the release of this book, and subsequent film, was that Haymitch teamed up with other recent victors like Beetee, Mags, and Wiress (as well as future gamemaker Plutarch Heavensbee) to try and take down the games from within. As we first learned that in Catching Fire, the flaws that Haymitch found in the arena contributed to his victory.

President Snow does not like to be beaten and finds creative ways to get revenge against those who double-cross him. We see that early on with Beetee’s son Ampert, who gets reaped to punish Beetee for how he won his games. We see it later with Peeta and Katniss. And Haymitch is allowed to return home at the expense of his mother, brother, and girlfriend. All three die horrifically as soon as he gets back. As many have said: Haymitch didn’t drink enough.

At the end of the film, we see how President Snow’s revenge against Haymitch’s community causes him to spiral fast. With his family and girlfriend dead, in addition to the PTSD from surviving the Hunger Games, he becomes inconsolable and addicted to sleeping syrup. It does not help that he is the only person living in Victor’s Village; because District 12’s only other victor, Lucy Gray Baird, went missing 40 years prior.

One day, Plutarch comes to visit Haymitch ahead of his victory tour to film an update for the Capitol News, and they have a chat. (In the book, this conversation happens during the tour.) He wants Haymitch to keep working with them to overthrow the Capitol. He refuses. “I am living proof the Capitol always wins,” Haymitch says. “You need someone different from me.”

Plutarch insists that they need someone exactly like him, actually. Even if it takes generations and they don’t get to see the results, they have to keep imagining a different future so that one day it comes true. It doesn’t make them heroes. It doesn’t even make them good people. But they’re trying.

Haymitch drifts away from two of his only friends, Burdock Everdeen and Asterid March. If you couldn’t tell from their names, or didn’t hear what they call their infant daughter, yes! Those are Katniss’ parents. (Also, yes! Burdock Everdeen is a member of the Covey. That’s why he sings one of Lucy Gray’s songs, "The Old Therebefore," at the funeral for District 12’s tributes. One day, he’ll change the course of history by teaching her “The Hanging Tree.”

The main reason that Haymitch cuts himself off is grief and guilt. He wants to be alone with his pain. Losing the people he was closest too makes him feel like he doesn’t deserve to be close to anyone, and maybe shouldn’t for their own protection. Movie protagonists are always thinking that, aren’t they? Like, okay Spider-Man! Whatever you say!

But the book reveals another, even more devastating reason: when baby Katniss grows up and starts wearing her hair in pigtail braids, she looks just like Haymitch’s “sweetheart” and first ally, Louella.

As we know from reading and watching The Hunger Games, the rebellion in Panem did eventually find the right victors to raise an army and end the games for good in District 12’s own Peeta Mellark and Katniss Everdeen. They were able to finally implement the plans that Beetee, Wiress, and the other victors had been testing since the second Quarter Quell, if not before.

Without even knowing it, Peeta and Katniss helped Haymitch keep his promise to Lenore Dove. So, of course, the story has to end with them. We see Haymitch, again played by Woody Harrelson, finally celebrating his birthday with no reapings. In Collin’s novel, Haymitch tells Katniss and Peeta about Lenore Dove in order to add her to a District 12 book of memories they’re putting together. Katniss gifts him goose eggs to hatch and raise. The film, however, takes a simpler approach, and just shows old man Haymitch greeting his former mentees before going to spend some alone time with the geese. He’s finally earned some peace.

Tom Hardy's Crime Thriller Hits 321 Million Minutes Watched on Streaming

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

MobLand has launched a brand-new era for modern gangsters on television. The series follows Harry Da Souza (Tom Hardy), the right-hand man and perpetual fixer for the London-based Harrigan crime family, led by Conrad (Pierce Brosnan), a brutal mob boss who reigns over the drug trade with terrifying violence. Even though Conrad is a frightening figure, his wife, Maeve (Helen Mirren), turns out to be even more ruthless.

Ritchie gives us a new look at London with the Harrigan family.

The Harrigans definitely keep it in the family, with sons Brandon (Daniel Betts) and Kevin (Paddy Considine), as well as Conrad's secret love child, Seraphina (Mandeep Dhillon), all competing to take over the head of the table. The family is constantly descending into dangerous territory, especially when a gang war breaks out in Season 1 between the Harrigan and Stevenson clans. Season 2, which kicked off in September, sees the Harrigans facing even more conflict, both from outside forces and their own internal fractures.

It's clear that MobLand is winning over audiences. The crime drama just hit a milestone of 321 million minutes watched on Paramount+ this year, and consistently lands in the top 10 most-watched streaming originals. Much of MobLand's success has to be attributed to the thrilling writing. The storylines are extremely fast-paced and action-packed, and with the backdrop of a treacherous criminal underworld, there's no shortage of high-stakes situations. MobLand also sets itself apart from other dramas by having morally ambiguous characters that you might find yourself rooting for, even when their actions are pretty abhorrent. Since no character is safe from death, the show is all the more bingeable.

Hardy leads MobLand's cast brilliantly as a man who has a strange set of ethics, making for a completely unique protagonist. Brosnan, in a role that's a far cry from his James Bond days, is harrowing as a man desperately trying to hold onto his power. But Mirren is on a whole other level as the Machiavellian matriarch of the Harrigans. Many of her actions are skin-crawling and cold-hearted, but Mirren ensures that Maeve is always endlessly fascinating to watch.

MobLand's supporting cast is every bit as worthy of mention. Joanne Froggatt plays Harry's aimless wife, Jan, who can't seem to understand how her life has become so entangled with a bunch of criminals. Lara Pulver, as Kevin's wife, Bella, nails the affectations of a bored wife who tries to use her position in the family to her advantage. Both Janet McTeer and Toby Jones have created memorable villains who use their intelligence, rather than brawn, to take down their enemies.

With Season 2 wrapping up this week, viewers who have invested in the show shouldn't worry — MobLand has already been renewed for a third season as well. As a crime thriller that thrives on chaos and danger, MobLand should continue its reign on streaming charts for a long time to come.

The First 8 Episodes Of This Detective Masterpiece Are Murder Mystery Perfection

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

As addictive as detective shows can be, it's an oversaturated genre, though one iconic British series stands above the rest — at least, its first season does. Just like the United States and its myriad police procedurals, Britain is chock-full of detective shows for every taste, from the cozy, long-running Midsomer Murders to gritty thrillers like The Fall.

It's impossible to talk about British murder mysteries without mentioning Broadchurch. Starring a post-Doctor Who David Tennant and a pre-Oscar-winning Olivia Colman, Broadchurch, which premiered on ITV in 2013, centers on the devastating murder of 11-year-old Danny Latimer (Oskar McNamara) in the titular English seaside town. A pillar of the community, local Detective Sergeant Ellie Miller (Colman) is prepared to lead the investigation, only for the job to be handed to the out-of-towner Detective Inspector Alec Hardy (Tennant).

A small-town killing and internal police politics may not sound like anything new, but Broadchurch season 1 took that familiar premise and turned it into something far more compelling, while Tennant and Colman's note-perfect performances elevated the series to masterpiece status. Unfortunately, Broadchurch's sophomore season was an undeniable step down in a few key ways.

Broadchurch season 1 is so spectacular because it takes an already riveting mystery and turns it into a complex, deep, and heartbreaking character study of a community in the throes of grief and how it fractures as a result. Rather than sticking Danny's family members and their fellow Broadchurch residents in neat procedural boxes like suspects, witnesses, etc., the show treats them as people reeling from loss and shows how easily close-knit environments can become pressure cookers when tragedy strikes.

The fact that Ellie's son was best friends with Danny adds another emotionally complicated layer to the story. Meanwhile, the killer reveal at the end of Broadchurch season 1 focuses less on the brilliant detectives who cracked the case, and more on how learning the answers doesn't always make things better, as deep-seated betrayal is a whole different beast than being left in the dark.

The 2010s were an intense time for TV with the advent of streaming, bringing incredibly acclaimed crime dramas to the table throughout the decade.

Unfortunately, the second chapter of the saga devolves into an awkwardly paced courtroom drama that sacrifices season 1's gritty realism for implausible theatrics. On top of that, Broadchurch season 2 introduces a new mystery that's not nearly as captivating as Danny Latimer's murder, making the sophomore installment feel scattered and unsatisfying. While it is mitigated by more excellent performances from Colman and Tennant, as well as supporting cast members like Jodie Whittaker, Charlotte Rampling, and Marianne Jean-Baptiste, season 2 is nowhere close to the perfection that is Broadchurch season 1.

Broadchurch's third and final season also falls short of season 1's magic, but it's a significant step up from season 2. Season 3 loses the unpopular courtroom storyline and shifts back to solely focusing on one case, the sexual assault of Trish Winterman (Julie Hesmondhalgh), while also finding closure for Danny's parents, Beth (Whittaker) and Mark (Andrew Buchan).

As a whole, when a TV show comes out of the gate swinging with a lightning-in-a-bottle first season, it can be incredibly difficult to sustain that momentum in subsequent installments, just as it can at times be impossible to recover from a dreaded sophomore slump. However, Broadchurch's ending came on the heels of a competent story that gave its characters fitting conclusions, righting the ship after the subpar season 2 and ensuring its legacy as one of Britain's best murder mystery shows remained intact.

'House of the Dragon's Final Season Is Officially 2 Years Away

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

He has covered everything from Marvel to the Oscars, and Marvel at the Oscars. He also writes obsessively about the box office, charting the many hits and misses that are released weekly, and how their commercial performance shapes public perception. In his time at Collider, he has also helped drive diversity by writing stories about the multiple Indian film industries, with a goal of introducing audiences to a whole new world of cinema.

It has been exactly one year, almost to the day, since HBO renewed House of the Dragon for a fourth and final season. The Game of Thrones spin-off premiered in 2022 and aired its third season earlier this year. Co-created by Ryan Condal and series creator George R.R. Martin — a partnership not without its rough patches — the series takes place hundreds of years before the events of the landmark HBO original. Since Game of Thrones' controversial conclusion in 2019, HBO has invested millions in expanding Martin's A Song of Ice and Fire universe.

Because of the long turnaround time for expensive productions, the final season of House of the Dragon is still some time away, according to HBO. Fans can, however, expect another Game of Thrones spin-off — A Knight of the Seven Kingdoms — to return with a second season "in the first half of 2027."

It has been observed that shows tend to lose viewers when they take too long between seasons, and while fans were accustomed to getting a new Game of Thrones season annually throughout the show's run, that pattern changed near the end​​​​​​​. Season 8 was released in 2019, two years after Season 7. It was also the shortest season in the show's history, consisting of just six episodes. However, the final season reportedly cost nearly $100 million to produce.

House of the Dragon debuted in 2022 and returned with a second season in 2024. The third season aired earlier this year. The latest season also received the best reviews of the lot and is now sitting at a "Certified Fresh" 91% score on Rotten Tomatoes. The aggregator website's consensus reads, "The fate of Westeros comes to a head in a reinvigorated and riveting third season of House of the Dragon, complete with wicked new characters and more thrilling battles, crafting a punchy prequel that matches the expectations of its predecessor."

Starring Emma D'Arcy, Matt Smith, and Olivia Cooke, the show will air its final season in 2028. The new season will begin filming in 2027, giving production around a year to complete work. HBO, now under the Skydance umbrella, intends to continue expanding the Game of Thrones universe. Aside from the second season of A Knight of the Seven Kingdoms, a big-screen film titled Game of Thrones: Aegon's Conquest is also being planned for a 2029 release. Stay tuned to Collider for more updates.

The reign of House Targaryen begins with this prequel to the popular HBO series Game of Thrones. Based on George R.R. Martin's Fire & Blood, House of the Dragon is set nearly 200 years before Game of Thrones, telling the story of the Targaryen civil war with King Viserys.

The Buccaneers had gotten close. The Lions have responded by pushing the margin back to 10.

The Lions now lead, 17-7. The Bucs are trying to muster an answer.

6 Iconic Movie Roles Nicholas Hoult Auditioned For But Didn't Get, Including James Bond

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Nicholas Hoult has spent more than two decades establishing himself as one of his generation's most versatile performers, building an impressive career that has seen him move effortlessly between independent cinema, prestige television, blockbuster franchises, and awards-worthy dramas. However, despite his considerable success, there are several occasions when Hollywood's biggest opportunities have slipped through his fingers, and that extends to James Bond 26.

From his early days as a child performer to becoming a genuine leading actor, Nicholas Hoult has demonstrated an extraordinary ability to reinvent himself. Audiences have watched him transform into everything from a troubled teenager to a post-apocalyptic warrior, a blue-skinned Marvel superhero, and a chilling historical monarch. His performances in About a Boy, Skins, Mad Max: Fury Road, The Favourite, The Great, and Nosferatu have showcased an actor with remarkable range, while his involvement in major franchises has made him an increasingly recognizable face around the world.

Hoult has frequently found himself in contention for some of Hollywood's most coveted characters. This has been most recently evidenced with reports that he was among the actors who screen-tested for James Bond, only for Robert Pattinson to ultimately secure the coveted 007 role. With Pattinson officially announced as the new Bond, Hoult has once again found himself on the wrong side of a major casting decision. But it's certainly not the first time. Here are six iconic roles Nicholas Hoult auditioned for but didn't get.

Before he was battling mutants or surviving the wasteland, Hoult could have been attending Hogwarts School of Witchcraft and Wizardry. Indeed, Hoult was among the hopefuls considered for the lead role in Warner Bros.' adaptation of J.K. Rowling's beloved novel Harry Potter and the Philosopher's Stone.

During an appearance on Hot Ones Versus alongside Nosferatu co-star Lily-Rose Depp in 2024, Hoult opened up about his childhood auditions for the franchise. He recalled attending several auditions and eventually meeting director Chris Columbus, even remembering wearing fake glasses during the process.

Hoult apparently got far enough into the casting process to audition directly with Columbus, although the director didn't remember their encounter when the subject came up years later. Ultimately, the role went to Daniel Radcliffe, who would become synonymous with the boy wizard across eight films.

Missing out on Harry Potter wasn't the end of Hoult's Hogwarts ambitions. In fact, the actor revealed that he was subsequently invited to audition for another major character in the franchise, Draco Malfoy.

The blonde-haired Slytherin antagonist ultimately became one of the series' most memorable characters, thanks to Tom Felton's performance. Initially introduced as Harry's schoolyard nemesis, Draco gradually developed into a more complicated character, particularly as the films explored his family's connection to Lord Voldemort.

Hoult recalled that, after failing to secure the lead role, he was asked whether he would audition for Malfoy. Unfortunately, that opportunity didn't work out either.

Interestingly, his recollection of the experience suggests that he kept auditioning for different parts throughout the casting process. As he jokingly explained, he was gradually auditioning for practically every role in the franchise until the filmmakers eventually decided against casting him.

Felton, meanwhile, would go on to become one of the franchise's breakout performers, with his portrayal of Draco remaining a fan favorite decades later.

One of Hoult's more recent casting disappointments involved another character with an enormous cultural footprint. Before becoming one of the most recognizable villains in the DC Universe, Hoult actually had his sights set on playing Superman himself.

When James Gunn began assembling the cast for his DC Universe reboot, Hoult emerged as one of the serious contenders for the Man of Steel. He was reportedly among the finalists for the coveted role, competing against David Corenswet, who ultimately secured the part.

The casting process even produced an amusing anecdote that Hoult later shared while appearing alongside Corenswet on Jimmy Kimmel Live! in June 2025. Hoult revealed that the pair had auditioned for Superman on the same day, with the actor recalling an encounter that made the eventual casting decision particularly memorable.

He described walking out of one of his audition scenes feeling reasonably confident, only to encounter Corenswet nearby. Recalling the moment, Hoult painted a rather cinematic picture of his future Superman co-star sitting in a ray of sunshine on the studio lot.

However, Gunn clearly wasn't ready to let Hoult leave the DC Universe altogether. Rather than losing the actor entirely, the filmmaker offered him the role of Superman’s bald-headed arch-nemesis, Lex Luthor.

If missing out on Superman wasn't enough, Hoult also came remarkably close to playing another of DC's most iconic characters.

When Matt Reeves began developing The Batman, Hoult emerged as one of the leading contenders to portray Gotham City's legendary caped crusader. The competition was particularly interesting because Robert Pattinson was also being considered for the role, setting up a showdown between two hugely talented British actors.

However, Hoult's experience with this particular casting process was somewhat unusual. During an appearance on the Happy Sad Confused podcast, he revealed that he actually learned Pattinson was expected to play Batman while listening to the radio in his car, approximately a week before his own screen test.

Understandably, the news was something of an emotional blow. Hoult recalled thinking that the casting hadn't been officially confirmed and that he still had an opportunity to prove himself.

Ultimately, Pattinson was selected, a decision Hoult subsequently acknowledged that he thought the decision was the right one, praising his fellow actor's performance.

Long before Game of Thrones became a global television phenomenon, Nicholas Hoult had an opportunity to join the fantasy epic as one of its most important characters.

During a 2019 interview with Page Six, the actor revealed that he had auditioned to play Jon Snow, the brooding member of House Stark who would eventually become one of the show's central figures.

At the time, Hoult was filming Clash of the Titans (2010), and his appearance for the audition was apparently rather unconventional. He recalled having long hair extensions, a ponytail, and a particularly patchy fake tan. In his own words, he remembered looking at himself and thinking that the appearance probably wasn't what the casting team had in mind.

Eventually, and unfortunately for Hoult, the role of Jon Snow went to Kit Harington, whose portrayal of the honorable, yet troubled warrior became one of the defining performances of the entire series.

For an actor who has spent years demonstrating his ability to lead major franchises, becoming James Bond would have represented perhaps the biggest opportunity of his career. Following Daniel Craig's departure from the franchise after No Time to Die, speculation surrounding the next 007 reached fever pitch. Countless names were thrown into the conversation, with Hoult among the British actors considered capable of inheriting the iconic role.

In November 2026, reports emerged that both Hoult and Robert Pattinson had been screen-tested for the part under director Denis Villeneuve. The two actors were among the later contenders in a competitive casting process, which also reportedly involved Callum Turner, Josh O'Connor and Jack Lowden.

Unfortunately for Hoult, history repeated itself. On November 16, 2026, Amazon MGM Studios officially announced that Robert Pattinson would become the next James Bond, succeeding Craig as the seventh actor to portray the cinematic spy. Pattinson's casting marks a major new chapter for the franchise, with Villeneuve directing and Steven Knight writing the screenplay.

Despite the inevitable blows missing out on so many iconic roles took on him, particularly James Bond 26, Hoult’s accomplished career demonstrates that missing out on iconic roles doesn’t necessarily mean missing out on success. Across a variety of roles, Hoult has cemented his status as an accomplished headlining star and formidable character actor.

"Bond 26" is the hitherto untitled 26th entry in the long-running spy action series following the titular James Bond and his adventures working for M16.

Explosion on Russian ship at Libya port caused by suspected drone attack, officials say

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Russian ship engulfed in flames after explosion at Libyan port

An explosion on a Russian ship docked at Benghazi naval base port was caused by a suspected drone attack, Libya's eastern government has said.

The explosion happened at dawn on Sunday, while the ship had stopped for fuel and supplies on its way back to Russia, Libyan state media reported, citing the eastern government.

It added that the site was secured, and there were no casualties. Moscow has yet to comment.

Satellite imagery from Planet Labs PBC, reviewed by BBC Verify, shows a boat on fire at 0547 GMT at the Benghazi port, with a separate image captured several hours later appearing to show the same vessel partially submerged.

Videos posted on social media and geolocated by BBC Verify show the ship engulfed in flames and smoke, while it is still dark.

US-based intelligence firm Vantor collected high-resolution images that it said on Saturday showed Project 20380 Steregushchiy-class corvette (Merkury) - a Russian navy ship - moored in the port, and on Sunday showed most of the same ship capsized and sunk with only the forward bow still visible.

Other satellite imagery from Planet Labs PBC showed a ship on fire at the port

The eastern-based Government of National Stability (GNS) said the Russian ship - which it did not specifically name or identify - was using the Benghazi port within the framework of an existing maritime agreement.

Sunday's incident is under investigation, and authorities are coordinating with their Russian counterparts to determine the source of the suspected attack and who is responsible for it, GNS added.

GNS condemned the attack on a ship inside a Libyan port, and called it a violation of maritime safety and an attack on their sovereignty. It said Libya was not party to the Russia-Ukraine war, or any other conflict beyond its borders.

Separately, Libya's internationally recognised government in Tripoli - the Government of National Unity (GNU) - warned against hosting foreign forces and allowing them to use facilities in a statement published by state media.

GNU said such activity raises "serious fears" about "risks that may result from involving ports and civilian facilities in activities related to external conflicts, and the resulting threat to the lives of citizens and the safety of national facilities and assets".

Libya fractured into violence and instability after its leader Muammar Gaddafi was overthrown in 2011, leading to the opposing governments.

Benghazi is more than 2,000 km (1,243 miles) away from the Russian capital Moscow.

Although it has not been officially confirmed who was behind the attack, there is little doubt, according to Jalel Harchaoui, Libya specialist at the Royal United Services Institute (RUSI).

"Ukraine is the only actor brazen enough to pull something like that off," he told the BBC. "If I were to bet my own money, I would bet that it was the Ukrainians."

If confirmed, it would not be the first time Ukrainian forces had carried out an operation of this sort. In March, Russia accused Ukraine of targeting the Russian tanker Arctic Metagaz in the Mediterranean between Libya and Malta with "uncrewed sea drones" launched from the Libyan coast.

As well as having an economic impact on Russia, hampering the ability of their so-called shadow fleet of tankers to export oil and gas, attacks like this are seen as humiliating to the Kremlin, according to Harchaoui.

But it is not only Moscow that will want to react to this latest incident. The warship was in the region as part of a deal with the government of eastern Libya - allied with Khalifa Haftar, commander of the Libyan National Army.

Harchaoui said Haftar will take the attack as a personal slight. "You're going to have a response by the Haftar family. They will ask questions about who is hosting the Ukrainians."

10 Greatest Film Noir Movies of the '80s, Ranked

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Film noir has been the go-to for compelling entertainment since the Golden Age of Hollywood — back when this genre was first coined in the 1940s and '50s, when stylish black-and-white classics like Double Indemnity, Sunset Boulevard, and The Maltese Falcon captivated audiences with their blend of crime and drama, becoming timeless staples of the genre for decades. Today, it's still a current filmmaking style that has a huge impact on pop culture.

There's no shortage of compelling film noir classics throughout the decade. Many, as mentioned before, are from the early years of its inception. But one decade has stood out spectacularly for reinventing the genre and incorporating its thrilling potential into the creative mix of the time period. That was the glorious 1980s, a decade marked by cinematic excellence, where blockbusters ruled, and it was just an absolute blast to be a movie buff. The 10 below define the 1980s with their unique use of film noir mixed with their equally complex stories, proving that this was a time marked by this genre.

Best described as a rock-and-roll action roller-coaster ride of 1980s awesomeness, Walter Hill's cult classic Streets of Fire takes neo-noir filmmaking to new extremes. This kick-ass flick just screams the '80s, featuring a thrilling soundtrack and a unique, rebellious style that perfectly encapsulates the time period. Michael Paré, Diane Lane, and Willem Dafoe lead in this memorable tale of a dangerous ex-soldier on a mission to rescue his singer girlfriend kidnapped by a biker gang and taken somewhere in a dystopian city.

Though a box office flop and critically mixed upon initial reception, Streets of Fire has garnered a cult following that many other '80s films could only dream of. It's stylish, one-of-a-kind, and above all, very entertaining, featuring a perfect mix of action, sci-fi, and film noir to provide viewers with an unforgettable experience. This embodies the youthful spirit and angst of '80s culture and tries to be nothing else.

Hitting all the right notes to make one of the more fascinating and memorable neo-noir classics of the decade, Peter Wier's 1985 film Witness is a must-watch for audiences due to how motivating and emotional its story beats are. Acting icon Harrison Ford steals the show as always, this time in, to date, his only Oscar-nominated performance as a Philadelphia police detective who infiltrates an Amish community to watch over a young boy who witnessed an undercover cop's murder.

The film is perfectly shot, written, and notably acted, as well as tightly paced, providing decent thrills, a compelling mystery, and enough drama and heart to satisfy just about anyone. Witness was a sleeper hit at the box office, racking in an impressive number, and, of course, was a hit with critics, citing its excellent blend of film noir and drama genres, and for its mature storytelling.

A darkly erotic, one-of-a-kind nailbiter, Brian De Palma's cult classic Body Double became one of the most original entries in the film noir genre to come out of the '80s. Though there's a heavy emphasis on shocking and risqué storytelling, it's much in line with a classic Alfred Hitchcock film, following a voyeuristic young man who's pulled into a bizarre murder conspiracy after spying on a beautiful neighbor one night outside an apartment window.

This has the makings of a classic suspenseful set-up, much like Hitchcock's Rear Window, which further explores the themes of voyeuristic obsession as this film does. Except, here the suspense is much more tense, the shock and violence really crank things up a notch, and the unique '80s style really sets it apart and gives it a charm. Body Double offers an edge-of-your-seat experience with great thrills and mysterious presence, making it one of the more fascinating film noir classics that perfectly represent this decade.

Featuring the triumphant feature-film debut of Academy Award-winning filmmakers Joel and Ethan Coen, as well as cinematographer and future director Barry Sonnenfeld and Oscar-winning actress Francis McDormand, the 1984 neo-noir crime thriller Blood Simple is quite simply one of the most underappreciated but masterful cult favorites to enter the decade. McDormand shone, showcasing what she would later give to more prominent roles in a twisted story of romance and murder as a scorned saloon owner hires a private investigator to kill his wife and the bartender she's been cheating with.

This unique, stylish thriller not only showcased the talent of its up-and-coming stars but also became a slow-growing fan favorite among movie buffs over the years for its tense pacing and great concept. It perfectly fits into the genre, complete with a decent mix of crime and drama to grip viewers and disturb them with its creeping suspense. Not to mention, it's a Coen film, which, of course, features their signature filmmaking style, which is visually stellar and hard not to appreciate. Overall, it's a must-watch that would delight any film noir fan.

Based on the legendary Thomas Harris novel Red Dragon, 1986's Manhunter was the first compelling look into the exciting yet terrifying world of the Hannibal Lecter series before becoming more mainstream when Jonathan Demme's The Silence of the Lambs shocked the world. Directed by Michael Mann (yes, the man behind Heat), it delivers a tightly paced, slow-building thriller which sees an ace FBI profiler, Will Graham (William Petersen), as he asks the infamous Dr. Lecter (Brian Cox) to assist in his hunt to catch the elusive Tooth Fairy Killer (Tom Noonan).

Though it's a whole different beast compared to The Silence of the Lambs, Manhunter is a perfectly made and epically gripping classic in its own right. The performances are near flawless, especially the late Tom Noonan as the killer, who can easily give audiences the creeps even now. Not to mention, its mystery and suspense are well-paced and build to a satisfying payoff, which has steadily made this film such a rewatchable and adored cult classic that's gotten more appreciation over the years.

Packing a powerful mix of neo-noir storytelling and tense suspense, William Friedkin's 1985 classic To Live and Die in L.A. provides cinematic excitement, which was what the '80s were all about. William Petersen brings his services again to the genre in this action spectacular, which sees him as a vengeful U.S. Secret Service Agent who crosses the line to eliminate the counterfeit artist (Willem Dafoe) who killed his partner.

From its uniquely '80s style, the kick-ass Wang Chung soundtrack to the heavy, awesome fight sequences and tense chases, To Live and Die in L.A. is an absolute blast of entertainment, one that's not just one of the best of the film noir genre, but just one of the most exciting features of the 1980s, period. It instantly hooks you in, never letting go for even a moment, and fascinates you with its tale of revenge and justice.

Truly one of the most remarkable, bold, and sexiest noir thrillers to come out of the '80s was 1981's Body Heat, a film directed by Lawrence Kasdan and starring the late William Hurt and Kathleen Turner. A neat, twisted little spin on the genre, which in itself is an homage to 1944's Double Indemnity, it follows a naive lawyer manipulated into a murder plot by a seductive femme fatale who has a few tricks up her sleeves.

It's tensely paced, perfectly acted, competently shot, and doesn't lose your attention for a second. Body Heat is a highly rewatchable masterwork that pays tribute to the classic film noir genre, as well as standing in its own right, telling a dark and alluring crime tale that has to be experienced. Just a few minutes of this classic can instantly hook you in.

Michael Mann returns, this time with arguably one of his greatest, most captivating works, Thief, a 1981 neo-noir thriller starring the late James Caan. It follows Caan as a professional thief who is on the path to turning his life around, but his pursuit of this dream becomes far more difficult as he desperately tries to get one last big score and deals with his old associates who are unwilling to let him leave his life of crime.

A critical and commercial success, Thief was instrumental in keeping film noir hot going into the 1980s. Today, it's regarded as one of the genre's most recognizable cult favorites. From Caan's nuanced lead performance as Frank to Mann's remarkable filmmaking talents, to the overall thrilling '80s feel of the film, it's an absolute delight that keeps getting better with age.

The one and only David Lynch fascinated countless fans with his unusual charm and one-of-a-kind storytelling. The film that arguably showed the greatest potential of his talents outside of Twin Peaks was 1986's Blue Velvet, a favorite among many Lynch enthusiasts for its captivating mystery and bizarre tone. Kyle MacLachlan stars as a curious college boy who returns to his old town and is quickly dragged into the town's dark criminal underworld after finding a severed human ear in a field.

It's certainly strange, off, and borderline nonsensical at a few points, but its mystery plot is straight to the point and undeniably fascinating. That's the best way to describe Lynch's masterpiece. Blue Velvet incorporates all his best attributes, and his story is a complement as well as something new to the film noir genre, leading it to become one of the best films of the year.

But truly, at the end of it all, there was no other place for Ridley Scott's 1982 sci-fi and noir masterpiece Blade Runner than at number one. It's honestly hailed as one of the most significant movies in cinematic history, a near-perfect film with flawless visuals, competent performances, and thought-provoking themes. Harrison Ford shines once again as a former cop turned detective-for-hire who is tasked with finding and killing a group of rogue androids who have rebelled against their creator.

This is a film that's still widely talked about and appreciated today for its brilliance and complex storytelling. There's no denying Blade Runner's impact on film noir. It not only breathed new life into the genre but redefined it in every way. This continues to influence the current genre, and rightfully takes this final spot as the film that most defines the film noir genre in the 1980s.

Mckenna Grace's New Dystopian Thriller Sparks 70k Petition Demand For 4-Hour Cut

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Fans are demanding more from Mckenna Grace's new dystopian thriller.

Grace began her career as a child actor around 2012 with early roles in Crash & Bernstein, The Goodwin Games, and The Young and the Restless. She found fame through her role in 2017's Gifted, as well as becoming known as the go-to "younger" version of major characters such as a young Carol Danvers in Captain Marvel. Now, the actress is beloved worldwide and has become a major star to watch across Hollywood, and is now appearing in one of the biggest releases of 2026.

A new petition has been launched, calling for the release of a four-hour cut of the movie The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping. The petition, on Change.Org, is nearly reaching 70,000 signatures after the movie's theatrical release on November 20. Director Francis Lawrence has been sharing certain scenes were cut from the movie, as fans begin noticing some moments from Suzanne Collins' original novel are missing from the final movie. The petition's starter, Kaitlin, shared a message that states:

"As an avid Hunger Games fan, I believe that Haymitch's story deserves to be told in its entirety. So many scenes from Sunrise on the Reaping were filmed but ultimately cut from the final movie, and I think fans deserve the opportunity to see them.

These scenes are important to understanding Haymitch's character, his relationships, and everything he endured during the 50th Hunger Games. There is so much more to his story than what can fit into a theatrical release, and it's disappointing knowing that so much footage exists that we may never get to see. Fans have waited years to see Haymitch's Games brought to life, and I truly believe his story deserves as much attention and detail as possible.

This petition is asking Lionsgate to release the full 4-hour cut of The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping, whether through an extended edition, streaming release, or physical media. These scenes have already been filmed. We just want the chance to see them and experience Haymitch's story as fully as possible!

Ladd McConkey will play against the Jets

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Chargers will have wide receiver Ladd McConkey in the lineup as they try to win their second game of the 2026 season.

McConkey was listed as questionable to face the Jets because of a back injury, but he avoided the inactive list 90 minutes ahead of kickoff in Los Angeles. McConkey has not played in the last two games.

Linebacker Daiyan Henley, cornerback Tarheeb Still, safety Kendall Williamson, guard Alex Harkey, wide receiver KeAndre Lambert-Smith, and wide receiver Brenen Thompson are inactive for the home team.

The Jets have scratched defensive back Jarvis Brownlee, wide receiver Omar Cooper, wide receiver Isaiah Williams, linebacker Trevin Wallace, tight end Jelani Woods, and linebacker Nate Lynn.

‘Madden’ Sparks Backlash From Raiders Owner Mark Davis Over Father Al Davis’ Portrayal: “That Hurts”

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

As John Madden‘s life gets the biopic treatment, his longtime colleague and friend Al Davis‘ family is not pleased with their late patriarch’s portrayal.

Las Vegas Raiders owner Mark Davis, whose father Al recruited Madden to coach for the NFL team when they were located in Oakland in 1967, criticized David O. Russell‘s Madden for its “totally unfair” depiction his father (portrayed by Christian Bale), who died at age 82 in 2011.

“It was full of turducken,” he told USA Today of the movie, referencing the Thanksgiving dish Madden popularized.

Mark continued, “As far as representations of my dad, yeah, that hurts, and I did the best I could to protect him. It’s absolutely a crime that they’re able to treat people that have passed away and characterize them in ways that are unfair, and there’s nobody around to protect their legacies. It’s just unfair. I don’t know what the purpose of the movie was to be real honest.” Watch on Deadline

He doesn’t consider his father “a pimp like Christian Bale played him, but that’s fine,” noting, “And I don’t blame Christian Bale. I don’t blame Nicolas Cage. All they did was what the director and the writers told them to do.”

Having screened the film two weeks before its release, Mark was surprised by a scene depicting the 1978 preseason game that paralyzed New England Patriots wide receiver Darryl Stingley, in which Al discourages Madden (Nicolas Cage) from seeing the player in the hospital.

“One of the main legacies of my father was how much he took care of people, whether it was on the Raiders or other teams,” said Mark. “People that were ill or needed help or whatever. He helped so many players on other teams that it’s incredible. And he did it quietly.

“And for them to put in the Darryl Stingley incident, that my dad was trying to talk John out of going to see him – that was just such a hurt to me personally, because that’s my father’s legacy, is how he treated people. No matter whether they were Raiders or not, he cared about people. That’s one of the redeeming qualities of my father throughout his life. And I just thought it was totally unfair for them to portray it that way,” added Mark.

While he said that scene “hurt” him, Mark added, “All the other crap, I could take all that (stuff). I don’t care if they talk about my haircuts and stuff like that. I don’t know what it had to do with anything, but I don’t mind that.  They can do that to me. But to do that to somebody that’s passed away, that can’t defend himself, is totally unfair. And I’m sure that there’s gonna be thousands of people that could contradict what that clip portrayed my father as.”

Mark added that the portrayal of his mother Carol Davis (played by Sienna Miller) and John’s wife Virginia Madden (Kathryn Hahn) is “all made up stuff too,” conceding, “But there are some, you know, elements of them in that. I will say that, but not much.”

Co-written by Russell and Cambron Clark, Madden charts John Madden’s career from Raiders coach to TV broadcaster to the namesake of the Madden NFL video game franchise.

Bills lead Dolphins 20-6 at halftime

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Bills have a big game against the Chiefs on Thanksgiving night, but they haven’t gotten caught looking ahead against the Dolphins this Sunday.

Josh Allen hit wide receiver DJ Moore for a touchdown and running back James Cook ran for another score as the Bills extended their lead to 20-3 in the second quarter. The Dolphins capitalized on a Josh Allen interception to cut the lead to 20-6 and the Bills’ bid to pad their lead appeared to end with an interception by safety Zayne Anderson with 18 seconds to play in the half.

Dolphins quarterback Kyle McCord returned the favor one play later, however. His pass to wide receiver Chris Bell was picked off by safety Damar Hamlin and the play proved to be doubly painful for the Dolphins when Bell needed medical attention after a big collision with Hamlin.

The Bills got the ball near midfield and Allen threw his third interception of the half, but it was wiped off the board by a defensive holding penalty. The Bills completed a short pass to Moore on the untimed final down of the second quarter.

Allen’s first interception came when Dolphins defensive end Chop Robinson tipped a pass that fluttered into the hands of rookie linebacker Jacob Rodriguez and Rodriguez took the ball back to the Buffalo 22-yard-line, but Greg Rousseau’s second sack of the game and his 10th of the season meant that they wouldn’t be able to turn it into six points.

It was an erratic half for Allen, who went into the break 15-of-30 for 173 yards, but a two-touchdown lead attests to the difference between the overall quality of the two teams on the field in Buffalo.

'GTA 6' Bombshell: Husband Melts Down After Wife Hid Her Role In Game For Years

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

A long-kept GTA 6 secret almost destroyed a marriage

The gaming industry is notoriously secretive, and that's doubly true for anything related to Grand Theft Auto 6. Rockstar Games kept much of the game tightly under wraps, including one of the title's biggest secrets — a remarkable feat when you consider how much of the game leaked prior to launch. All of this might be true, but it didn't take the sting out of one husband's recent discovery about his wife's work on the record-breaking open-world title.

Amber Wallin is a comedian who voices several non-playable characters in GTA 6. Wallin's voice can be heard during an early segment of the game where Lucia is notified she will be allowed to carry out her sentence via a quasi-home arrest program. Wallin appears in a few other roles throughout the game, such as a character who roasts Jason after he harasses her.

Given her profession, it's no surprise that Wallin's characters are a hoot. "Motherfucker wanna clock out soon when shit gets real," she says via the aggravated NPC. "Clock in, ho. Time is money!"

While Wallin's various roles are minor, the game is already the biggest entertainment launch of all time, meaning anyone who can say they were involved in its creation has much to brag about. So, imagine the surprise of Wallin's husband when he learned she was in the game like any other player. According to a video the couple recently posted on social media, Wallin apparently kept her involvement in the game a total secret from even her husband for two years.

"NDAs do not apply to husbands," Wallin's partner screams. The video is not entirely serious — at one point, Wallin suggests Rockstar Games could assassinate her for talking about it even now. The clip was obviously planned beforehand and is staged to look like a dramatic segment in a reality TV show as well. Yet the feelings expressed in it, and Wallin's tight-lipped approach to her work, appear to be 100% genuine.

Perhaps the most ironic part is that Wallin has a video where she playfully describes her relationship with her husband. In it, she describes a time during pregnancy where she took care of a handful of tedious house chores. When she was finished, she wanted to treat herself with a pint of ice cream — but when she opened the fridge, all she found was an empty carton. On top of not throwing it out, the husband evidently did not even bother to put the ice cream back in the freezer.

Given GTA 6's focus on relationships and how much it encourages men to be thoughtful partners, perhaps playing through the game will be instructive and remind Wallin's husband that he has NPCs at home.

Players weren't expecting that keeping Lucia happy would require any actual work

Why Wiress From The Hunger Games: Sunrise On The Reaping Looks So Familiar

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

This article contains spoilers for "The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping."

The original "Hunger Games" raised the stakes for Katniss Everdeen (Jennifer Lawrence) and Peeta Mellark (Josh Hutcherson) by establishing that tributes from their home, District 12, have only ever won the Hunger Games twice in 74 years. That means they only have one mentor, the alcoholic Haymitch Abernathy (Woody Harrelson), unlike other Districts' tributes that have two mentors each.

"Hunger Games" author Suzanne Collins told the story of the first District 12 victor in "The Ballad of Songbirds & Snakes" — Lucy Gray Baird (Rachel Zegler), who won the 10th Hunger Games decades before Katniss was born and then vanished. Now, "Sunrise on the Reaping" tells how Haymitch won his own Hunger Games.

Haymitch was at a disadvantage before he even entered the arena, since there were no District 12 victors available to mentor him. To address this issue, the Capitol "borrowed" victors from other districts and assigned them to mention Haymitch and his fellow District 12 tributes. Those mentors are Mags Flanagan (Lili Taylor) and Wiress (Maya Hawke), younger versions of characters who first appeared in the second "Hunger Games" film/book, "Catching Fire."

Wiress (played by Amanda Plummer back in "Catching Fire") isn't in the movie much, but "Hunger Games" fans will likely recognize the actress playing her. The daughter of Ethan Hawke and Uma Thurman, Maya Hawke is one of the more charming "nepo babies" to come out of recent Hollywood.

Hawke has been acting in film and television since 2017, when she played Jo March in a BBC production of "Little Women." In 2019, she not only appeared in Quentin Tarantino's "Once Upon a Time in Hollywood" as "Flower Child," a member of the Manson family; she also got her big break. Hawkey joined "Stranger Things" in its third season as Robin Buckley, working alongside Steve Harrington (Joe Keery) at mall ice cream store Scoops Ahoy.

The season seems to set up Robin and Steve as a new romantic couple ... until she reveals she's not into guys! Despite the show taking place in the 1980s, though, Steve is cool with it. While Season 3 of "Stranger Things" tends to be remembered as one of its weaker outings, Robin was a great addition to the ensemble and Hawke returned as part of the main cast for the subsequent two seasons.

Hawke has also worked alongside her father. In historical miniseries "The Good Lord Bird," where Ethan Hawke starred as radical abolitionist John Brown, Maya Hawke played Brown's daughter, Annie. When Ethan Hawke directed the Flannery O'Connor biopic "Wildcat" in 2023, he cast Maya as the famed author.

Maya Hawke's other film roles include parts in Wes Anderson's "Asteroid City," Pixar sequel "Inside Out 2" as the voice of Anxiety, and a part in the upcoming "Magic: The Gathering" film. Hawke is also a singer-songwriter and has released four studio albums ("Blush," "Moss," "Chaos Angel," and "Maitreya Corso") as of 2026.

"The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping" is now in theaters.

Mahershala Ali Says It ‘Doesn’t Matter’ That He’s Won 2 Oscars Because ‘There Are Not Roles for Me’ in ‘the Vast Majority of Projects’: ‘You’re Like Everybody Else Waiting’

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Mahershala Ali got candid about the impact — or lack thereof — his two Oscar wins have had on his career in Hollywood while appearing on a recent episode of Carmelo Anthony and Kazeem “Kaz” Famuyide’s “7PM in Brooklyn” podcast.

The actor, who took home trophies for best supporting actor for both 2018’s “Greenbook” and 2016’s “Moonlight,” asked the hosts about the meaning of an Oscar, and if it really validates whether someone is “a good actor” or not. “There’s more good actors than there is good material,” he said. “It’s not even that all these people are competing for these same spots. It’s just that out of all the material made, there’s only a handful of them that check certain boxes.” Related Stories Stage Adaptation of Amanda Seyfried, Sydney Sweeney-Starrer 'The Housemaid' Sets London Premiere Lionsgate Quarterly Revenue Pops 41% on Strength of 'Michael,' TV Series Sales as Studio Narrows Net Loss

He continued: “The vast majority of projects that get greenlit, there are not roles for me in those. You gotta think that way, you know what I mean? So, it doesn’t matter if I got 17 Oscars. If someone is not writing something for you or greenlighting something with you in mind, you’re like everybody else waiting and looking and searching.”

While the star noted that actors could produce their own projects, the timeline is usually as least a “three to five year” journey. When asked by Famuyide if an Oscar win validates Black actors, Ali responded: “It’s kind of just talk. It doesn’t translate to anything real. You don’t want to find yourself in a situation where, oh, that’s ‘the diversity project.’ You just want to do great material as a Black person. You’re not thinking of yourself as this exotic other, per se. You just are living your life.”

Most recently, Ali starred in Bassam Tariq’s “Your Mother Your Mother Your Mother,” which hit theaters on Sept. 25. Tariq was also the original director of Marvel’s failed “Blade” movie, in which Ali was set to star in the title role, which he recently discussed in an interview with The New Arab.

“It was funny and strange to start casting and building without a proper script,” Tariq told interviewer Hanna Flint about his short-lived Marvel career. “You’re sitting there and explaining to actors what their role will be, and they just have to believe you. That’s also very dangerous because if the text comes and it doesn’t add up to what you were saying, your credibility gets completely shot. All I have is my integrity, and I’ve been thinking about that a lot.”

“If you’re the bearer of bad news to your actors, but you’re not the one making any of these decisions — and it’s actually coming from a corporate entity — that’s not a great way to be a director,” he continued. “I know some people can take that and run with it, but I learned I was losing credibility with my actors, and it wasn’t a healthy way to operate as a filmmaker. Life’s too short to make shit films, so that was my big lesson: I have to have full control over my script. If we disagree, it’s between them and me. It’s quite sad because we had such high hopes for what we wanted to build.”

Speaking of the film’s inability to get made, Marvel Studios president Kevin Feige had previously said he felt “like a gigantic loser and failure,” a statement that Ali agreed with in a GQ interview in the months following. “To this day, I don’t know why it didn’t happen. I just know that it was—Kevin, he said it was the biggest failure of his career and I agree,” said Ali.

New Dungeons & Dragons Live-Action Series Officially Replaces Netflix's Forgotten Realms

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Mists are apparently rolling into Netflix.

A live-action Dungeons & Dragons series based on Ravenloft is in development at Netflix, giving the tabletop gaming franchise’s most famous horror setting its own shot at TV. The project, simply titled Ravenloft, comes from screenwriter John August, Alfonso Cuarón, and Hasbro Entertainment, and is described as the successor to Netflix's other, now-canceled D&D project, The Forgotten Realms.

While Dungeons & Dragons is often associated with taverns, dragons, treasure, and quippy adventuring parties, Ravenloft is the game’s gothic nightmare corner. It’s the place where D&D trades high-fantasy heroics for haunted castles, cursed lands, tragic monsters and the sense that there's always something lurking in the fog.

Ravenloft has been one of D&D’s most iconic settings for decades, dating back to the original 1983 adventure built around Count Strahd von Zarovich. Strahd remains the setting’s defining villain — he's a vampire lord, tragic monster, and tyrant trapped in the mist-shrouded land of Barovia.

The setting later grew into the Domains of Dread, a collection of horror realms ruled by Darklords who are often imprisoned in worlds shaped by their own sins and obsessions. The premise gives the TV show a lot of flexibility in the stories it tells, bouncing between gothic horrors, cursed folklore, and actual nightmares within the one setting.

That flexibility is a big part of why Ravenloft has endured. While Curse of Strahd remains the best-known modern entry point for many D&D players, newer books like Van Richten’s Guide to Ravenloft and Ravenloft: The Horrors Within have expanded the setting beyond Barovia, turning it into a toolkit for nearly every flavor of horror D&D can support.

According to a Deadline report, the Ravenloft project is also part of a broader push to expand D&D outside the core tabletop game. Netflix had also been developing The Forgotten Realms, though Deadline reports Ravenloft is now positioned as its successor. Meanwhile, a sequel to Dungeons & Dragons: Honor Among Thieves remains uncertain, even though co-writer and co-director Jonathan Goldstein recently said a script exists.

D&D’s reach has also been boosted by projects that don’t look like traditional tabletop products. People are still playing, modding and arguing about Baldur’s Gate 3 years after its 2023 launch, while Critical Role has helped turn actual play into a major entertainment lane of its own, with live shows, animated adaptations, and a huge audience beyond the table.

Ravenloft: The Horrors Within is a good update of Van Richten's Guide to Ravenloft, despite some typical 5.5e design choices

Star Wars' TV Spinoff Connected To Andor Characters Encouraged By Show Creator

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Andor creator Tony Gilroy hopes that the Star Wars series will receive its own spinoff.

In 2022, six years after Rogue One: A Star Wars Story was released, Disney and Lucasfilm pointed the spotlight on Diego Luna’s Cassian Andor, one of the movie’s main characters. Andor chronicled the five years of Cassian’s life before he died on that fateful mission to steal the plans for the first Death Star. Though fans know how his story ends, Gilroy would like to see characters introduced in the acclaimed Star Wars TV show be explored further.

During an interview with Polygon, Gilroy encouraged the powers-that-be running the Star Wars franchise to move forward with such a project. He pointed out how at the end of the Andor series finale, Adria Arjona’s Bix Caleen was shown holding a baby, with the implication being that this was Cassian’s child. Gilroy explained:

I don't see how it cannot affect it canonically. We built a lot of canon over 24 hours. I mean, we stuck to canon a lot, but we created a lot of IP, a lot of characters, a lot of different things. I left them with a baby. One would think that at some point someone's gonna continue that.

By the time Andor’s two-season run ended in 2025, many of its important characters had been killed off, including Stellan Skarsgård’s Luthen Rael, Kyle Soller’s Syril Karn, and Varada Sethu’s Cinta Kaz. However, along with Bix, there are still a handful of characters from the series who could hypothetically be brought back for post-Rogue One and A New Hope stories. This ranges from Denise Gough’s Dedra Meero languishing in an Imperial prison, to Elizabeth Dulau’s Kleya Marki potentially going on more missions for the Rebellion.

Because Gilroy’s time in the Star Wars franchise is over, any kind of Andor TV spinoff would need to be handled by another creative. Gilroy noted that he’s not “not in constant communication with Lucasfilm” anymore, so he doesn’t know if such a project is being considered, let alone worked on. Nevertheless, from his perspective, this Andor-centric corner of the Star Wars universe is rife with possibilities for expansion, particularly when it comes to to Bix’s baby.

Although Andor ultimately changed from an original five-season plan to a two-season show, showrunner Tony Gilroy says he doesn't feel any absences.

There are currently only two upcoming Star Wars TV shows officially slated. Ahsoka season 2 will arrive in early 2027, reuniting viewers with Rosario Dawson’s Ahsoka Tano in the post-Return of the Jedi era when Lars Mikkelsen’s Grand Admiral Thrawn has resurfaced. Star Wars: Maul — Shadow Lord season 2 is also expected to premiere sometime in 2027, with that show following Darth Sidious’ former apprentice attempting to rebuild his criminal empire following the conclusion of the Clone Wars. Most recently, all eight episodes of Star Wars: Visions Presents — The Ninth Jedi were released on August 5.

Andor can be streamed in its entirety on Disney+ with the rest of the other Star Wars movies and TV shows. Along with Ahsoka season 2 and Shadow Lord season 2’s imminent arrivals, Star Wars: Starfighter, the franchise’s next movie, will be released on May 28, 2027.

‘Harry Potter’ Meets ‘The Fugitive’ in the Fantasy Classic Officially Dominating Streaming

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

There's just over a month to go until the doors to Hogwarts open again and HBO's new Harry Potter adaptation begins. Despite all the controversy surrounding the Wizarding World's creator, J.K. Rowling​​​, which is sure to rage on as the series continues, excitement is as high as ever for the return of one of the biggest fantasy franchises of all time.

A recent clip of the series, showcasing the new students tucking into their first feast in the Great Hall, has earned 1.8 million views in just four days, even if it has caused some backlash online for its color palette and lack of floating candles. The clip did give fans a closer look at some of the main cast in action, including Dominic McLaughlin, Alastair Stout, and Arabella Stanton as Harry, Ron, and Hermione, respectively, Janet McTeer as Minerva McGonagall, and John Lithgow as Albus Dumbledore, with the latter already earning plenty of conversation on social media.

The hype for Harry Potter is unsurprisingly sending fans back to the original film series. At the time of writing, the time-travel-based third story in the franchise, Prisoner of Azkaban, is one of the ten most-watched movies on Prime Video worldwide. The film follows the manhunt of Gary Oldman's wrongfully imprisoned Sirius Black, similar to the iconic Harrison Ford thriller The Fugitive, and is widely considered the greatest film in the series.

After Chris Columbus laid the foundations of the Wizarding World in the first two installments, acclaimed visionary director Alfonso Cuarón took over for this third film, giving viewers the most immersive experience in the whole franchise. Cuarón reshaped Hogwarts, connecting notable locations with dynamic camerawork so that the entire castle felt more real than ever.

The film's quality was repaid at the box office, with a reported $130 million budget turned into a global haul that fell just short of $800 million. Split between a domestic haul of $251 million and a further $533 million from overseas markets, Harry Potter and the Prisoner of Azkaban became the third-highest-grossing film of 2004, falling short of Spider-Man 2 ($797 million) and Shrek 2 ($933 million).

Harry Potter and the Prisoner of Azkaban is streaming now. Stay tuned to Collider for more.

Recasting Sex And The City For 2026: All 8 Main Characters

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Faith Roswell is a Senior Writer on Screen Rant's Classic TV team. Since earning her degree in Creative Writing over a decade ago, Faith has written articles on film and TV from a variety of different angles. Faith now combines her knowledge of psychology with her love of monster movies to give more insight into what makes the best ones.  You may have read her Screen Rant lists and features covering horror, sci-fi, and fantasy, or read her Amazon Top 10 book, "Movie Monsters of the Deep." Faith has had an extensive career as a writer, appearing on BBC live radio, researching true crime for Rotten Mango podcast, and writing for publications including Mental Floss, Atlas Obscura, and The Daily Jaws before beginning here at Screen Rant.

Sex and the City is one of the iconic TV shows that defined the 1990s, changing the way we talk about and show sex and relationships onscreen, with this discourse continuing today in a very different way. The TV series has aged so badly that this has overshadowed just how unique it really was, but it acts as a time capsule to the 1990s, which gives it a place in both TV and cultural history, with its four main characters still remaining discussion topics today.

Sex and the City's main characters, Carrie, Samantha, Miranda, and Charlotte, were revolutionary, talking about sex and love in a way that no other mainstream TV show had put onscreen, and the four have remained distinctive style icons even during the show's ill-fated reboot. And Just Like That tried to undo some of the worst aspects of Sex and the City, including the characters' unintentional bigotry and lack of diversity, but this was far from a successful approach, and a better one may have been to reboot the series from the start, with new actors exploring modern subjects.

Already no stranger to working with HBO, Issa Rae's work on Insecure has established her as an actor more than capable of embodying the stylish mess that Carrie Bradshaw really is. Carrie sacrifices necessary aspects of living, like her kitchen, in favor of buying shoes, has an enormous wardrobe of striking looks, appears impossibly elegant, and yet is a crumbling mess underneath it all, desperate for acknowledgment from Mr. Big.

Rae's Insecure character, who shares her name, thrives on romantic insecurity, channeling heartbreak into creativity through her signature mirror raps, much as Carrie uses her relationship failures as fodder for her column. Even setting Insecure aside, Issa Rae's best movies and TV shows have such a range that it is easy to imagine her in Carrie's Manolo Blahniks, whether she is falling over in the street, at home working on her column, or dealing with truly devastating events.

Samantha Jones has grown more controversial over time for her misogynistic beliefs and willingness to enter taboo relationships, including with teenagers and Charlotte's brother. An updated version of Samantha would be sexually adventurous rather than predatory, with the viewer exploring newer relationship types through her. This would require an actor who could embody her defiance and flexible sexuality while feeling more authentic than Kim Cattrall when delivering Samantha's often-quoted line, "I'm a try-sexual. I'll try anything once."

Tessa Thompson came out as bisexual in an interview with Net-A-Porter, and is a vocal champion for the LGBT+ community. Casting her as Samantha would allow her controversial relationship with artist Maria to feel natural rather than deliberately provoking, but there is more to Thompson than her sexual orientation that makes her perfect for the role. Her characters in Hedda and Westworld show her commanding a room as Samantha would working as a publicist, and she has the dramatic range to make Samantha and Smith's relationship timeline truly magnetic, from the initial meeting to her entire cancer and recovery storyline.

Phoebe Waller-Bridge might be inescapably British, but this could be written into an updated version of Miranda Hobbes, the power-dressing lawyer whose struggles to break the glass ceiling give her some of the best lines in Sex and the City. A rebooted Miranda could be well-traveled and world-weary, bringing the signature wit and dry British humor Waller-Bridge used to make Fleabag into a masterpiece. Fleabag covers some of the same ground as Sex and the City, but is certain to age better.

Any actor taking on a role like Miranda must put their own stamp on the character rather than merely emulating Cynthia Nixon's performance, and Waller-Bridge has an expressive face that would allow Miranda to say exactly what she is thinking even when the focus is on the other characters. Miranda is sometimes her own worst enemy, and needs to remain a sympathetic character even when she is continuing a pregnancy she doesn't want, and taking back a cheating husband, and if anyone can continue to win viewers over even while her character makes seemingly incomprehensible choices, it is Waller-Bridge.

Lily Collins' effortless "Old Hollywood glamour" style and physical resemblance to Charlotte York has resulted in her often being fan-cast for the role. That said, a successful reboot needs more than a lookalike and Collins' previous roles have also resembled aspects of Charlotte, proving her easily capable of playing her. Emily in Paris showed Collins playing a hopeless romantic with a striking fashion sense, without the character feeling forced.

Her Golden Globe-nominated role in the romantic comedy Rules Don't Apply involves her exploring a potential relationship with a person she is not supposed to, and navigating her rigid religious and life rules in much the same way as Charlotte does when she meets Harry. While Rules Don't Apply ends differently, the movie feels like a trial run for Collins as Charlotte, who must decide whether she would rather wait for a hypothetical fairytale or choose the man she fell in love with.

Daniel Radcliffe might be an unconventional choice to play Charlotte's true love, Harry Goldenblatt, but he has a history of playing unconventional characters, which makes him a surprisingly perfect fit. Having worked tirelessly to step out from the shadow of Harry Potter, Radcliffe has shown his acting range in strange movies and TV shows, and while Sex and the City is considerably less weird than movies like Horns, playing Harry would highlight Radcliffe's ability to play a more subtle character too.

The original main Sex and the City characters and their actors

Radcliffe has appeared in sitcoms before, with a cameo in Unbreakable Kimmy Schmitt, and his character in the movie The F Word showed him as a believable romantic hero. Harry is one of the best partners in Sex and the City, being warm, genuine, and true to himself, and these characters fit with Radcliffe's natural demeanor. His Jewish heritage brings essential cultural grounding to the character, as Harry's religion and culture feature in some of his best storylines.

Of all the love interests in Sex and the City, Steve has aged like milk. Originally portrayed as a sweet and enthusiastic man whose confidence and charm allowed him to pursue Miranda, finally winning her heart, rewatching Sex and the City tells a very different story. Steve is immature, pushy, and deeply insecure over Miranda's success, and yet the character is an extremely flawed human rather than being an outright villain, and he must not be portrayed as one. This requires an actor who can balance these aspects of his character, making his emotional vulnerability show through the mask.

Steve Brady is almost a perfect reversal of Joe Keery's best-known character, Stranger Things' Steve Harrington. While a much younger character, Harrington is popular, sporty, and insecure, but has a moment of reckoning that turns his weaknesses into strengths and wins over both viewers and his love interest. Steve Brady would give Keery the chance to play an alternate universe version of Harrington, in which his petulance overrides his charm, while still being central to some of the show's funniest moments.

Smith Jerrod is one of the few men in Sex and the City who got a character arc almost as strong as the central four women. When Samantha meets the struggling actor working in a restaurant, she works her PR magic, transforming him into a genuine movie star, falling for him in the process. Regé-Jean Page has the movie star quality to carry off Jerrod's transformation, and his charisma was central to his romance storyline in Bridgerton, proving him capable of playing a character who could even make Samantha fall in love.

Sex and the City has 70% and 81% scores on Rotten Tomatoes from critics and audiences, respectively.

Since Bridgerton, Page has been somewhat typecast as a romantic hero through roles like Michael in You, Me, & Tuscany, but while Sex and the City's Jerrod gets a fairytale ending, the character would give Page much more to work with. Jerrod is also a recovering alcoholic who finds New York's alcohol-centered party atmosphere and overenthusiastic fans difficult to cope with, and this story could be given a greater focus in a rebooted version of the show.

To a modern viewer, Mr. Big is a sleazy millionaire who has no intention of committing to Carrie, no matter how desperately she asks him to. However, the archetype is a fixture of 1990s culture, making it essential that a modern version of Sex and the City updates the character, not to redeem him but to make him recognizable to new viewers. Kieran Culkin's character in Succession, Roman Roy, is an ideal modern version, masking his insecurities with sarcasm and crudeness.

Carrie and Mr. Big's relationship is the core of the show, with everything she does ending up leading back to him, which is presented as a happy ending in the context of the series. This would almost certainly be the hardest part to sell in a reboot, making Mr. Big's casting one of the most important. Culkin has made a career out of playing unlikable characters without turning them into outright villains, and if he could balance Mr. Big's worst characteristics with his occasional flashes of genuine kindness, the character might have a chance at carrying the series, even now.
