John Wayne Almost Played Clint Eastwood's Dirty Harry

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

​​August is a freelance film writer. She studied journalism and cinema at American University. She is a member of the journalism honor society, Kappa Tau Alpha. August has written for various networks and publications, including PBS, Gargoyle Magazine, TheThings, and Identity Theory Magazine. Before returning to entertainment writing, August worked in the Automotive section of U.S. News & World Report. In her free time, August enjoys watching movies in theaters, watching movies at home, listening to podcasts about movies, reading books about movies, watching video essays on movies, talking about movies, thinking about movies, and doing crosswords.

Hollywood film experienced a major shift in the 1970s, following the fall of American idealism and Golden Age of Hollywood. Traditionally optimistic genres such as westerns and musicals waned in popularity while gritty crime-dramas and conspiracy flicks took center stage. This left traditional Hollywood stars in a state of flux, perhaps having to transition into more ambiguous roles. Such changes were deemed necessary even for the likes of John Wayne, who nearly stepped into a major role that now feels tailor-made for Clint Eastwood.

John Wayne was on top of Hollywood for an incredible length of time. Shortly after his leading role in Stagecoach, Wayne gradually evolved into the face of the Western movie genre. He starred in a multitude of high-quality Westerns in the 1940s, and his career exploded further in the 1950s, with the actor making some of the best movies of his entire career, including The Searchers and The Quiet Man.

While still a period of success, the 1960s were a bit quieter for John Wayne, whose specific brand of Westerns gave way to the grittier films made popular by Spaghetti Westerns like Clint Eastwood and Sergio Leone's Dollars Trilogy. In the 1970s, Wayne adjusted to the changing demands of Hollywood, and that approach could very well have landed him in the role of a now-iconic crime thriller.

While Clint Eastwood famously held the titular role, he was not the first name in the running. Western legend John Wayne was reportedly considered for the role of Detective Callahan in Dirty Harry. There are conflicting reports concerning his reasons for turning down the role, or whether he was actually considered for the part at all. Despite some mixed information, it is undeniable that Dirty Harry would be a completely different film with Wayne in the lead.

Several Hollywood icons were reportedly in the running to lead Dirty Harry, including George C. Scott, Paul Newman, Frank Sinatra, and John Wayne (via Far Out Magazine). Sinatra was said to have signed on until a hand injury forced him to withdraw. Reports suggest that Wayne was next on the list, but turned down the role. Warner Bros. released a press release on December 17, 1970, announcing Eastwood as Harry Callahan in the coming drama (via AFI).

There are several reasons as to why Wayne would decline such an opportunity. Michael Munn's 2001 biography, John Wayne: The Man Behind The Myth, quotes Wayne as admitting that the decision came down to pride and dissatisfaction with the character. He was reluctant to accept what he considered “Sinatra’s rejections.” He was also reluctant to inhibit a crooked cop character, which went against his morals and Hollywood persona. However, as the book notes, he later came to realize that he had played rogue heroes before, in Westerns such as The Searchers.

"I turned it down for what seemed to me to be three very good reasons. The first is that they offered it to Frank Sinatra first, but he’d hurt his hand and couldn’t do it. I don’t like being offered Sinatra’s rejections. Put that one down to pride. The second reason is that I thought Harry was a rogue cop. Put that down to narrow-mindedness because when I saw the picture I realized that Harry was the kind of part I’d played often enough; a guy who lives within the law but breaks the rules when he really has to in order to save others.”

Outside of Wayne's own feelings about the role, both he and Sinatra were also considered too old to play Harry. Wayne was a good 15 years older than Eastwood. For this reason, some sources suggest that Wayne was never officially offered the titular lead.

Dirty Harry may not have achieved such astronomic success without Eastwood helming the cast. The actor possessed the intensity and edge that Wayne lacked.

While both Wayne and Eastwood came from a Western background, Eastwood was more versed in playing lawless anti-heroes. Wayne gained popularity in traditional Westerns of the 1940s and ‘50s, portraying generally altruistic figures. Meanwhile, Eastwood entered the genre during the Spaghetti Western period in which morally grey protagonists were in vogue. He played anti-heroic leads in hits such as 1966’s The Good, the Bad and the Ugly.

There is little question about how Wayne would have played Harry Callahan, as he later starred in two Dirty Harry imitations—McQ and Brannigan. In both instances, he seemed ill-suited to the rogue-cop archetype. Wayne read as a man entrenched in a traditional American idealism, clearly uncomfortable with the crime genre's random violence. In contrast to Clint Eastwood, he was ultimately stiff and unconvincing, contrary to the commanding presence he exudes in his Westerns.

Quentin Tarantino’s Definitive Masterpiece Officially Returns as a Streaming Hit Ahead of Sequel

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

There aren't many directors with as clean of a resume as Quentin Tarantino, who has won several Oscars despite directing fewer films than many of the directors that belong to his same generation. It's been a while since Tarantino had anything new to direct, but he is set to return to the screen later this month with a new release, The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth, which stars Brad Pitt and Yahya Abdul-Mateen II. Tarantino wrote the script for the film, but he did not direct, relying on David Fincher (Se7en, The Social Network) to helm the film. Earlier this month, Fincher confirmed that he had to cut dozens of pages from the script Tarantino first delivered him, citing that the material he wrote was closer to two movies than just one.

The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth is a direct sequel to the 2019 film, Once Upon a Time... in Hollywood, which stars Leonardo DiCaprio opposite Brad Pitt. Tarantino even helped Brad Pitt win his first acting Oscar for his performance in the film, which is partially why his return to the role later this month has so much motion behind it. Seven years after leaving theaters, Once Upon a Time... in Hollywood has found a streaming home on Prime Video, where it's bounced in and out of the top 10 for over 100 days this year. For those without a subscription to Prime Video, the film is also available to stream for free courtesy of the Roku Channel, which doesn't even require a subscription or account for its users. There are no shortage of ways to watch Tarantino's original Hollywood masterpiece.

A few weeks ago, when promotion for The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth was beginning, Quentin Tarantino confirmed that he had written a script for a movie he'd like to direct, but he was waiting for the right time. He declined to share any details about the film, and while it was once thought his next (and possibly final) movie would be about a film critic, he has seemingly left this idea behind. Every filmmaker has the right to work on their own timeline, but most fans would agree Tarantino is wasting his golden years instead of spending his time doing what he does best: making movies.

Check out Once Upon a Time... in Hollywood on Prime Video or the Roku Channel, and stay tuned to Collider for more updates and coverage of Quentin Tarantino's future and past projects.

Prime Video's 'The Rings of Power' Is Making a Truly Unprecedented Move With Season 3

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Editor's note: The below contains spoilers for The Rings of Power Season 3.

The Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power is one of Prime Video's biggest shows, thanks to its massive budget and sprawling world, but the fantasy series is only getting bigger. The show has already featured several cinematic moments, like the eruption of Mount Doom or the Sack of Eregion, and Season 3 promises to take things to a new level. Prime Video is releasing Episode 7 in theaters, allowing fans to see the story in a new way. It's a bold step for the streamer, but it could be a major moment for The Rings of Power.

Season 3's penultimate episode will depict an important event in Middle-earth's history, adding to the significance of The Rings of Power's first theatrical release. In addition to being the first episode on the big screen, Episode 7 will come to theaters early, on November 23, raising expectations. However, fans who can't make it to theaters will still get to watch the conclusion sooner than expected, as Prime Video will make both Episode 7 and Episode 8, the finale, available for streaming on Tuesday the 24th rather than following the season's original Wednesday schedule.

The Rings of Power Season 3 has a lot going on: Gandalf (Daniel Weyman) is in Rhûn trying to save the Dark Wizard (Ciaran Hinds), Elrond (Robert Aramayo) is helping to resolve the civil war in Númenor, the Elves are in open war against Sauron (Charlie Vickers), and Durin (Owain Arthur) is fighting his own brother. Yet Episode 6 tees up a major discovery for Sauron that promises to change Middle-earth entirely. Episode 7's theatrical screenings have left little doubt about what the plot will feature, and the creation of the infamous One Ring deserves a bigger viewing experience. Fans have been waiting for this, as Season 2 left only one ring yet to be made, and Sauron has been slowly working toward achieving that goal.

While Sauron's success is inevitable, informing Frodo's story hundreds of years later, accomplishing the creation of the One Ring hasn't been an easy process. In Episode 6, Sauron tricks Celeborn (Jamie Campbell Bower) into revealing the secret of the Elven Rings' craftsmanship by impersonating Galadriel (Morfydd Clark), setting up the forging of the One Ring. Despite its significance, the moment it was made has never been depicted onscreen before, making Episode 7 the perfect choice for a theatrical release.

Many episodes of The Rings of Power would be a spectacle on the big screen, but Season 3's penultimate hour deserves this opportunity. Prime Video gave The Boys' series finale a similar treatment earlier this year, and other popular series, like Stranger Things, have done the same. However, Prime Video is taking a slightly different approach with The Rings of Power by choosing not to release the Season 3 finale in theaters. The Rings of Power is also set to run for two more seasons, giving it even more time to grow beyond this crucial Middle-earth moment.

Prime Video's choice capitalizes on the franchise's name recognition, especially with the story now reaching this landmark scene. Between J. R. R. Tolkien's books and Peter Jackson's beloved film franchise, virtually every fantasy fan knows about the One Ring, but, more importantly, The Rings of Power is making a deliberate choice to prioritize Season 3's penultimate episode based on its story importance. The best episodes of many epic shows aren't their finales, and the most action-packed episodes are the ones that deserve a theatrical experience. As TV series grow, there is something to be said for releasing them in theaters, and now is the perfect moment for The Rings of Power to make its way to the big screen.

5 Actors Who Should Play M In Robert Pattinson's James Bond Movies

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The next era of the "James Bond" film franchise is finally taking shape. After years of wondering who will be suiting up as our new 007, Robert Pattinson has officially been cast as the next James Bond, taking over for Daniel Craig following his final entry with "No Time to Die," which came out way back in 2021. A lot has happened since then, including Amazon buying the rights to the property. Now, with a new lead actor in place, it's time to get the rest of the pieces of the puzzle locked in. That includes finding Bond's boss.

Historically, this has been the one they call M. Originally portrayed by Bernard Lee in the earliest Bond films, the codename given to the person behind the big desk at MI6 is an important one, as they head up the 00 program. Thus, M is the one who gives Bond his assignments and, at times, even helps him out in the field. It's an important role, one that has been played by the likes of Dame Judi Dench and Oscar-nominee Ralph Fiennes.

There's much to be done. Director Denis Villeneuve also has to find an actor to play the villain in Pattinson's "James Bond" movie, but M is a longer commitment, a multi-picture deal. It's a big responsibility. So, who should play the role alongside Pattinson? Who should be the one to help guide 007 through his next several missions for her majesty's secret service?

We're going to go over a handful of options, ranging from very obvious to unlikely yet intriguing. Let's get into it.

"The amount of time I have wanted to be M. I'm not sure who I need to call..."

Those are the words of Olivia Colman speaking to British Vogue in 2023. She wants the role. She's very well qualified for the role. If everyone wants to make this easy, why not just offer her the role? Sometimes, the easiest answer is also a very good answer. That not every actor who campaigns openly for a role should get it, but we're not just talking about any old actor here.

Colman is responsible for one of the most shocking Oscars upsets of all time when she took home Best Actress for "The Favourite." But it was more shocking because the Academy recognized Colman's impressive work over the rest of the pack. Ever since, she's been on a tear, turning in outstanding performances in everything from "The Lost Daughter" to "Paddington in Peru." Heck, she even ate up some screen time in Marvel's otherwise very disappointing series "Secret Invasion."

But Colman has been doing it at a very high level for years, long before she won an Oscar. "Hot Fuzz," "The Iron Lady," "Broadchurch." You name it, Colman is one of the best parts of everything she's in. Beyond that, she could bring both a seriousness to the role of M, which is required, while at the same time allowing for a playful dynamic with Bond. Colman feels like a slam dunk. It just so happens that the person campaigning for the role is very much right for the role. That's not always the case.

If Denis Villeneuve or Amazon want to go with a distinguished, male British actor who could be older and wiser than Robert Pattinson's Bond, one capable of being both convincingly stern and charming, Colin Firth is the man. An Oscar-winning actor who is almost too easy to picture sitting at a big desk behind a padded burgundy door, this is another "the obvious choice can also be the right choice" pick.

We have an actor here capable of delivering exposition, as M often has to, in a way that would be entertaining, as Firth could read the phone book and it would be engaging. But let's not forget that Firth politely destroyed fools in "Kingsman: The Secret Service," too, and that movie owes a lot to the "James Bond" franchise. What's more, it proved that Firth looks absolutely fantastic in an expensive suit, an important quality of a good M.

His resume speaks for itself. "A Single Man," "The King's Speech," and even "Mamma Mia!" prove that Firth is one of the finest actors of the last 30 years. There's zero question as to whether or not he's qualified to be M. So, it begs the question: Is he right for M? If we're looking to address that question in the traditional sense, then the answer is a resounding yes. The shoe fits and it fits well. Simple as that.

If it weren't for Judi Dench playing M so damn well for so damn long, we might have to have the utterly irritating "let's gender-swap M" conversation. Fortunately, that's not the world we live in. Bearing that in mind, it's going to be damn hard for anyone to argue against Tilda Swinton bossing around Robert Pattinson while dressed to the nines. What's not to like about that?

Swinton won a Best Supporting Actress Oscar for "Michael Clayton" so, much like many of the other performers on that list, she's Oscar-caliber. That almost feels like a must for M. Setting that aside, Swinton brings a domineering, powerful quality to so many of her best roles. Even so, she's a bit of a chameleon, which is partially what would make her an interesting M. It's not hard to imagine her playing an MI6 agent who used to work in the field before taking a desk job, yet she still has that darkness behind her eyes from yesteryear.

Oddly, it's Swinton's transformation into the Ancient One" in "Doctor Strange" that might make the best case here. She can do powerful yet quiet without having to be at the center of the action, and she can absolutely handle being a part of a giant, globally popular franchise. Really, there's nothing this role requires that she couldn't do, seemingly with ease.

Some might argue Swinton would be better as a "Bond" villain. That certainly wouldn't upset me, but it might be subverting expectations a bit to have her as Bond's buttoned-up boss. She could also help bring something fresh to the role for this new era.

And now we arrive at the most obvious answer. Does that make it the wrong answer? Using history as our guide, absolutely not. Ralph Fiennes made his entry into the "James Bond" franchise in 2012's "Skyfall" before fully taking over as M in "Spectre" following the death of Judi Dench's M. (May she rest in peace.) So, why replace a perfectly great actor in the role if we don't have to?

Let's not forget that the "James Bond" franchise is notorious for bringing actors back in roles even after reboots. Dench as M for both Pierce Brosnan and Daniel Craig's Bonds. Desmond Llewelyn was Q for every Bond except Craig. That's the nature of the beast as it exists. If Amazon doesn't totally buck tradition, it could easily bring back Fiennes for another go-around. And why not? He's already proven he can do it, he's a stellar actor, and he's arguably as popular as ever.

He's also an actor who seems to really understand the role. "Spectre" was going to make M the villain until Finnes refused. That, to this longtime fan, demonstrates a real understanding of who M is. On top of that, Fiennes is very familiar with shouldering the weight of a major movie franchise, having previously played Voldemort in the "Harry Potter" movies. The only reason to not do this is if Amazon and Denis Villeneuve truly and completely want to hit the big red reset button, which frankly seems likely.

But if not? The debate could be settled before it even gets started.

Last, but certainly not least, we arrive at the choice that's both decidedly out-of-the-box yet technically in the very box we're talking about. Pierce Brosnan previously played James Bond in four movies stretching from 1995 to 2002. His "GoldenEye" remains the most important box office hit in "James Bond" history, in addition to being a beloved entry in the franchise. But Brosnan's run was uneven and ended on a sour note with "Die Another Day." Perhaps playing M would give him a shot at redemption?

Let's get some things out of the way up top. Yes, it might be a little weird to have a former 007 play M, but it's not that much stranger than fully ignoring continuity in order to have the same actor play M for multiple Bonds. More importantly, Brosnan absolutely looks the part. The man, well into his 70s, still looks better in a suit than most of us could ever hope to. He's got an authoritative on-screen presence, but he's also got that charm that could work wonders for M.

Brosnan feels he was never good enough as James Bond. I would humbly argue that the scripts weren't always good enough for his James Bond, not the other way around. Why not use the past to help build a future for the franchise? Let Brosnan bring his experience to the role and mentor Robert Pattinson's 007. The meta nature of it all might add something to their dynamic.

The question of whether or not Brosnan would want to take on the role is another one entirely, but it's a wild card idea that's just inspired enough to be a whole lot of fun.

The new "James Bond" movie doesn't have a release date, but stay tuned.

Quentin Tarantino's Western Thriller Masterpiece Is Officially Free on Streaming

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Brian VanHooker is a Pop Culture Writer specializing in Celebrity Interviews and In-Depth Oral Histories in the areas of Comedy, TV, Film, Cartoons, Comic Books, 80s/90s Nostalgia, Fatherhood, Retro Games and Food (especially Pizza). He was the Senior Features Writer at Cracked and a Staff Writer at MEL. He has written for Inverse, Inside Hook, Maxim, Men's Health, Fatherly, SFGate and more. He's the co-creator of the comic book/cartoon Barnum & Elwood and The Tramp, an award-winning comedy pilot starring John O’Hurley. He also hosts a TMNT interview podcast called Turtle Tracks and was once called a “Good Guy” by Mr. T.

As far as Quentin Tarantino movies go, just about everyone loves Pulp Fiction. Reservoir Dogs and Once Upon a Time… in Hollywood are also widely regarded, while the Kill Bill films and Django Unchained each have a pretty fervent fanbase. But with so many great entries in his filmography, it’s inevitable that at least a few of his movies would get lost in the shuffle. One of those often overlooked films is his 2015 Western thriller, which may not be top-tier Tarantino, but it’s still an excellent film that combines the strengths of two of his previous entries.

The Hateful Eight, which is currently free to stream (with ads) on Tubi, takes place in the 1870s in the wintry Wyoming Territory. Kurt Russell is John "the Hangman" Ruth, a bounty hunter bringing in Daisy Domergue (Jennifer Jason Leigh), a gang member with a big price on her head. He’ll collect his bounty in the town of Red Rock, with a stopover at a place called Minnie's Haberdashery on the way, but Ruth’s driver (James Parks) has to stay ahead of an impending blizzard.

On the way to Minnie’s, Ruth’s carriage picks up two unexpected passengers, both of whom lost their horses in the snow and will surely die in the blizzard. First there’s Major Marquis Warren (Samuel L. Jackson), a fellow bounty hunter and former Union Army general​​​​​​​ who keeps a personal letter to him from Abraham Lincoln in his breast pocket. The second unexpected passenger is Chris Mannix (Walton Goggins), the new sheriff of Red Rock and the son of a Confederate war hero. While Ruth is suspicious of both of them, he decides to let them join him on the way to Minnie’s.

Once they arrive at Minnie’s, however, Minnie isn’t there. Instead, the haberdashery has apparently been left​​​​​​​ in the care of a Mexican man named Señor Bob (Demián Bichir) while Minnie is visiting her mother. Also present are three other men​​​​​​​ that Warren, a frequent guest of Minnie’s, doesn’t recognize. As the entire group is snowed in for the night, it’s eventually revealed​​​​​​​ that nobody is quite who they say they are and bloodshed ultimately ensues.

Like I said, The Hateful Eight is not top-tier Tarantino. Tarantino’s best work has generally come when he’s charting new territory for himself. Inglourious Basterds​​​​​​​ was his first war movie, and with its heist-like plot and unabashed joy in rewriting history, it showed what Tarantino could do with that genre. Django Unchained was his first Western, and, with its heavy influence from Spaghetti Westerns and Tarantino’s trademark talkative, over-the-top characters and depiction of extreme violence, it showed what a Tarantino Western looks like.

The Hateful Eight doesn’t do that. In fact, it can pretty easily be summed up in classic Hollywood this-meets-that fashion: The Hateful Eight is Django Unchained meets Reservoir Dogs.

It being another Western with broad, loud, talkative characters, it has the same feel as Django Unchained. Originally, The Hateful Eight was a Django sequel called Django in White Hell, but Tarantino found that Django didn’t fit the story. And while Django isn't in it, it’s clear that this movie is in the same ballpark.

As for the Reservoir Dogs part of it, it is narratively similar, with it being tightly contained primarily in one location, with a bunch of untrustworthy characters who don’t really know each other very well. It’s also a bit of a whodunit, just like Reservoir Dogs is with its “Who’s the rat?” plot thread.

None of this is to say that The Hateful Eight isn't a good movie — not at all. The Hateful Eight is an expertly crafted film which begins with a slow burn and delivers surprise after surprise once the bullets start flying. The movie blends a lot of genres, with elements of a Western, a thriller, and even a bit of broad comedy, but it's most effective as a mystery. Not only does the plot slowly unravel as Ruth tries to figure out who he can trust, but each of the characters does, too​​​​​​​.

Speaking of those characters, there are no good guys in the movie, but there are a lot of bad guys you end up rooting for because they are incredibly entertaining to watch. Samuel L. Jackson’s character is joyously funny, and Kurt Russell’s hangman is a mean grouch with a strange but consistent set of principles. Also, Walton Goggins, who was previously in Django, racks up another memorable Tarantino character in this movie. (Let’s hope he shows up in Tarantino’s 10th flick and takes a break from those damn Walmart commercials already).

While it stays firmly within previously charted territory for Tarantino, The Hateful Eight is very entertaining and very funny. It has all the hallmarks of a great Tarantino movie, with lots of fun characters, over-the-top violence, and unexpected plot twists. It may not be top-tier Tarantino, but it’s a really good time.

Egyptian Music Star Mohamed Mounir Gets Biopic – Doha Industry Days

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The life and legacy of legendary Egyptian artist Mohamed Mounir is set to be explored in a biopic spearheaded by The Film Committee at Media City Qatar.

The Film Committee has acquired the rights to document Mounir’s life story, with partners on the project set to include a leading regional studio and other partners, which have yet to be unveiled.

The project was announced at the Film Committee’s Doha Industry Days, just hours after the Qatari body gave an update on previously announced biopic Caesar, about Syrian whistleblower Farid Al-Madhhan, announcing Taim Hasan as it star. Related Stories News 'Al Hayba' Star Taim Hasan To Play Syrian Whistleblower Farid Al-Madhhan In Neon-Backed 'Caesar' Biopic News Steven Paul Talks Up Federal Film & TV Tax Credit: "It's Going To Be Good For The World" - Industry Days Doha

Mounir’s career spans nearly five decades across music, film and television. Watch on Deadline

His 1981 breakthrough album ‘Shababeek’ established him as one of Egypt’s most distinctive artists, with his work characterized by its fusion of Nubian folk and Arabic pop with Western genres like jazz, blues, and reggae.

He is known to his fans across the Middle East and beyond as “El King” in reference to his 1998 album and play ‘El Malek Howwa El Malek’ ((The King is The King).

Also working in cinema, his some 20 credits including collaborations with late Egyptian filmmaker Youssef Chahine on films including Egyptian Story (1982) and Destiny, which played in Out of Competition in Cannes in 1997 and was then selected as Egypt’s Oscar entry.

Mounir was born into a Nubian family in a village in Southern Egypt, where he spent his early childhood. His family moved to Cairo, after the village was resettled due to the construction of the Aswan Dam in the 1960s, where his singing voice was discovered when he sang at a family gathering.

The Film Committee said the biopic will trace Mounir’s journey from his humble beginnings to becoming one of the Arab world’s most celebrated and influential artists, revealing untold details, surprises, stories and anecdotes.

“Mohamed Mounir’s music holds a special place in the lives of audiences across Egypt and the Arab world,” said Film Committee at Media City Qatar Chair Hassan Al Thawadi.

“Bringing his story to the big screen is an opportunity to celebrate his artistry, the cultural heritage his work represents and its lasting social relevance, a story that connects generations of fans across the region and has the power to resonate with audiences around the world,” he continued.

“This project also builds on our recent investment in extraordinary life stories from the region and reflects our ambition to develop a world-leading slate of biographical films that brings these stories to audiences globally.”

Alongside the feature film, plans include a behind-the-scenes documentary featuring interviews with Mounir and those who have lived and worked alongside him, as well as documenting the development and production of the biopic.

With development in the early stages, the hunt is now on for an actor to play Mounir.

These 8 Single-Season Fantasy Shows Are Masterpieces

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Fantasy TV is greatly known for thriving on sprawling worlds and long-running sagas, multi-season chaos that offers thrilling character journeys. But sometimes the genre produces rare and fantastic hits that deliver just a single season. That one-season hit often wields an unforgettable story with ambitious themes, striking visual direction, and an emotionally satisfying arc—even when it concludes on a slight cliffhanger.

Masterful series like the layered superhero drama, Watchmen, that produces an alternate version of history, and the cult classic gem, Neverwhere, that explores a hidden magical London, are just two series in the genre that demonstrate just how brilliant a one-season fantasy icon can truly be. Compiled on this list are the TV shows that wield the title of masterpiece, despite their single-season status.

Over the Garden Wall is an animated show that may wield a short runtime, but the series' fairytale-like storytelling and haunting visuals ensured its lasting legacy as a masterful fantasy masterpiece. The Cartoon Network series follows a pair of lost brothers, Wirt (Elijah Wood) and Greg (Collin Dean), who stumble into "the Unknown," where they meet several unusual characters on their strange journey home.

Over the Garden Wall somehow captures wonder and melancholy together perfectly. It's an animated series that’s beautifully strange and skillfully atmospheric. Over the Garden Wall is a truly unique ride that is presented like a traditional storybook tale, but with an eerie atmosphere, darker tone, and escalating moral stakes. What makes it a masterful one-season watch is that every episode acts like a "chapter" that still advances the character and theme: adolescence vs. childhood, fear vs. hope, and the seduction of giving up when the going gets tough. Over the Garden Wall is definitely a fantastic fantasy great that stands as a tightly crafted masterpiece despite its status as a single-season series.

This brilliant fantasy series is one of the finest of the genre that has ever been brought to screens, and unfortunately, only has one season to its name. My Lady Jane is an alternate-history fantasy, focusing on the bold and ambitious Lady Jane Grey (Emily Bader), who is forced into marriage and involuntarily thrust into lethal court politics.

My Lady Jane is a reimagining of English history with a playful fantasy twist. The show's premise is genuinely intriguing as it delivers magical lore and thrilling political drama. My Lady Jane's energetic storytelling and clever take on the past make it an extremely entertaining single-season masterpiece. Without withholding its payoff to later seasons, the show commits to a full season-long story arc, including political affairs, romance, and a heady tone. It is truly unfortunate that My Lady Jane never got the chance to expand its story due to its cancellation, but it does make for a great one-season fantasy gem.

Watchmen is an expansion of the iconic comic universe that transports audiences into a daring alternate-history fantasy drama. Set in a world where masked vigilantes are treated as outlaws, the series focuses on detective Angela Abar aka Sister Night (Regina King), as she investigates conspiracies that reawaken old ideological violence.

Watchmen is a quality limited series that honestly outdoes those with a much longer run time. The show's bold themes, stunning performances, and meticulous writing prove that with just one season, a story of excellent caliber can be brought to audiences. Watchmen stands as a powerful fantasy jewel that easily earned itself a strong reputation as a television masterpiece. While using genre spectacles like superheroes, dystopia, and mystery to do precise thematic work, the show builds on the usual comic-book tropes and turns them into a sharp social commentary. Watchmen is often lauded as one of the best miniseries to be produced by HBO, solidifying it as the perfect one-season epic for this list of masterpieces.

This Marvel TV series is a skillful blend of classic television nostalgia and epic superhero fantasy. WandaVision initially begins with superheroes, Wanda Maximoff (Elizabeth Olsen) and Vision (Paul Bettany), as they live a happy, sitcom-shaped suburban life as a married couple. It isn't very long before their world begins to fracture, as the two start to notice strange happenings that seem to have a single source—Wanda.

WandaVision is initially an incredibly quirky series that honestly stumps most audiences who are watching it for the first time. But it doesn't take very long to see why most viewers are quick to praise the iconic series. WandaVision hosts inventive storytelling and fantastic emotional depth that elevate its one-season arc into a fully realized fantasy masterpiece that's truly able to captivate audiences. Its story may be short, but WandaVision ranks high amongst some of the absolute best in the fantasy genre.

Four worlds and four completely different ideas of what a good life looks like. This is where you belong, what it would ask of you, and what you would get back in return.

You belong somewhere old enough that the mountains have names and somebody can still tell you who gave them. Middle-earth is for people who think the right thing is worth doing even when it will not be finished in their lifetime.

You want the sea, a crew, and absolutely nobody telling you where you are allowed to go. The Grand Line is the world for people whose main ambition in life is finding out what is over there.

You would rather be feared and useful than liked and useless. The Continent is a world where the monsters are the straightforward part of the job and the people are where it all gets complicated.

You want the myths to be real and loose in the actual world, and you want to find out at about thirteen that you were never anywhere near as ordinary as everybody kept telling you.

Lovecraft Country is an intriguingly fearless mesh of horror, fantasy, and historical commentary. Set in 1950s America, the story centers on Atticus Freeman (Jonathan Majors) as he, along with Letitia "Leti" Lewis (Jurnee Smollett) and Hippolyta Freeman (Aunjanue Ellis-Taylor), travels across the country in search of his missing father, only to encounter not only racist terror, but also supernatural monsters.

Lovecraft Country is a rather bold series with a very entertaining concept; unfortunately, it never made it far, as it was cancelled rather quickly. Despite that, the show is good enough as a one-season gem that delivers an incredible story. With a combination of American history and cosmic horror, Lovecraft Country offers a unique narrative that is significantly memorable despite its status as a single-season series. From production design to music choices, Lovecraft's cultural work only makes its story feel all the more historical and mythic, marking it as the perfect addition to this list of masterful one-season watches.

This unfortunately cancelled series came and left in a burst of creativity and thrill. The Bastard Son & The Devil Himself is a book-to-screen coming-of-age adaptation that follows the young Nathan (Jay Lycurgo), who finds himself caught in between warring witch clans as the son of a feared killer.

The Bastard Son & The Devil Himself is extremely addictive, but horrendously underrated. It's a one-season masterpiece for its intriguing story and fantastic performances. The Bastard Son & The Devil Himself is genuinely entertaining, and its abrupt cancellation definitely disappointed many of its fans. Even with the dark fantasy's brief run, The Bastard Son & The Devil Himself, with its gripping storytelling, has earned its place among some of fantasy's most overlooked masterpieces, making it the ideal pick for this list of peak gems in the vast genre.

Neverwhere is based on Neil Gaiman's novel of the same name and stands as one of fantasy’s greatest cult favorites. The beloved series centers around Richard Mayhew (Gary Bakewell) as he helps an injured girl named Door (Laura Fraser), only to find himself erased from ordinary London and pulled into the hidden fantasy underworld known as "London Below."

With an imaginative premise and a thrillingly eerie atmosphere, Neverwhere is a stunning fantasy masterpiece that delivers a mysterious underground realm filled with hidden magic and strange characters. The show's tightly contained narrative and inventive world never feel like it's merely a one-season story; instead, Neverwhere offers audiences a fully realized narrative. The 1996 series further showcases its masterpiece status with vivid worldbuilding, clear stakes, and a complete transformation of its protagonist. Neverwhere does wield that BBC flair, but the series is only enhanced by it, giving it a dream-like atmosphere. The series concludes with a resolution that feels like a quality payoff, marking it as a bingeable watch that delivers a complete fantasy masterpiece that’s worth diving into.

This iconic horror series may not be in the realm of traditional fantasy, but the supernatural gem is a ghostly fantasy drama that definitely counts as a memorable single-season masterpiece. The Haunting of Hill House follows the fractured Crain family, who are forced to relive the trauma of their haunted childhood home as the story switches between the past and present, showcasing how the family's adult lives are shaped by what they survived inside "Hill House."

The Haunting of Hill House wields a chilling blend of supernatural horror and family drama, masterfully delivering a series rife with great scares and surprising amounts of emotional depth. The show is a limited series, so it feels like it's built perfectly for a single season. Each episode is both a puzzle piece and a character study—a genuine full thesis statement. With fantastic direction and incredible production design, the overall craftsmanship of The Haunting of Hill House only proves that a single season show can still offer audiences quality entertainment.

GTA 6 Launch Stats Are In: Over 50% Of Active Console Users Played

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

GTA 6 has already outgrossed the entire box office run of Avengers: Endgame, the highest-grossing movie in history — and the sheer scale of that success is represented in the launch day stats.

According to Mat Piscatella, senior director and video game industry advisor at Circana, over 50% of all active consoler users played GTA 6 at launch, averaging 494–513 minutes of playtime, or roughly 8 hours.

That does not mean 50% of all console users played it.

"For players active on November 19th, 61% of US PlayStation 5 and 56% of global Xbox players engaged with Grand Theft Auto 6," he posted to Bluesky. "PS5 GTA 6 players averaged 513 minutes that day, Xbox players 494. Both the DAU and minutes played engagement rates are absolutely bonkers."

Piscatella doesn't have the exact stats to hand, explaining that it's "not exactly easy to go back and sort daily active engagement rates," but to his recollection, during his time at Circana, there has been "nothing near this."

The only game second to GTA 6 on launch was Fortnite, accounting for 10% of all active players across both platforms. Clearly, the Pirate Shop Access tokens are working.

As for what GTA 6 players were up to before the game launched, Piscatella had some additional stats, sharing the top-five games between November 6 and 19:

$3.7 billion in revenue within 24 hours, "the biggest entertainment launch of all time," accounting for over 50% of all active console users — those numbers are nothing to scoff at.

In fact, in less than a week, with 32.5 million copies sold, GTA 6 is already one of the best-selling games of all-time. It currently sits on the edge of the top 30 after overtaking Super Mario Odyssey, Borderlands 2, and Monster Hunter: World.

A lot of people felt compelled to buy something they could play GTA 6 on after being shown a lot more of it.

Considering that GTA 5 is the third best-selling game of all-time, behind only Minecraft and Tetris (clearly, the secret to selling hundreds of millions of copies is blocks), it's easy to imagine GTA 6 landing somewhere near the top.

Almost six years after the PS5 launched, almost two million people are still playing GTA on PS4.

Indian Auteur Girish Kasaravalli Looks Back on 50 Years of Cinema at IFFI Masterclass: ‘All My Films Talk About Othering’

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Indian auteur Girish Kasaravalli looked back on his career in Kannada-language cinema at the 57th International Film Festival of India (IFFI) in Goa, in a masterclass titled “The Moral Universe of Parallel Cinema: 50 Years of Girish Kasaravalli.”

The 75-year-old spoke about the moral contradictions of society and the relationship between cinematic form and social content. He also argued that even entertainment is a political act. The session was moderated by Variety‘s Asia editor Naman Ramachandran. Related Stories Oscar-Nominated Director James Longley Talks His Afghanistan Documentary ‘Mohabat’ and Why His Next Film Will Center on the Rohingya Genocide (EXCLUSIVE) India’s ‘How Love Moves’ Among Top Winners at IDFA Industry Section Awards

Across films such as “Ghatashraddha” (1977), “Tabarana Kathe” (1986), “Thaayi Saheba” (1997), “Dweepa” (2002) and “Gulabi Talkies” (2008), Kasaravalli has kept returning to one question: how societies exclude individuals and communities, and how people who are themselves victims of oppressive structures can also become complicit in perpetuating them.

Asked whether the 1977 Kannada classic “Ghatashraddha,” which examines religious orthodoxy and social exclusion, still spoke to contemporary India, Kasaravalli said: “All my films talk about othering. I always talk about how society looks at some people from a different lens, and in that process they make them ‘other.'” He described this as “the biggest cruelty an individual or a group of people can impart on another person.”

Asked whether he considered himself a political filmmaker, Kasaravalli said: “Whoever makes something, whether he makes mainstream cinema or art cinema, he is making a political statement, either in support of the dominant ideology or opposing the dominant ideology. He cannot avoid it.” There is nothing called only entertainment, he added, and even entertainment is a political form.

On his characters, he said: “I don’t want any of my characters to become a kind of a mouthpiece to a certain ideology.” Drawing on Jean-Luc Godard, he compared understanding a character to buying a house: one must examine it from the outside and then from within before making a judgment. Similarly, a filmmaker must inhabit a character and then step back to assess it. For Kasaravalli, characters are too complex for a filmmaker to easily take sides.

On the evolution of his cinematic language, Kasaravalli described the visual construction of “Ghatashraddha,” as “ritualistic cinema.” “The style is not imposed on it; it comes from within the content itself,” he said. Since “Ghatashraddha” was about a ritual, he felt its cinematic form should reflect that structure.

Kasaravalli said he could not write a script until he had clarity about its images. “For me, the film is not merely making statements; it’s an attempt by the filmmaker to impart an experience.” He described writing detailed instructions for his crew, down to which hand a character uses to lift a glass, because even that changes the composition of a shot.

This attention to the relationship between character and environment shaped “Gulabi Talkies.” Kasaravalli told cinematographer S. Ramachandra that the coastal community had to be as important as the protagonist. He wanted the images to feel like “actuality coverage,” not a predetermined visual construction. Rejecting conventional scenic shots of the sea and rocks, he told his cinematographer: “Film should not have any pretty image.” They chose 16mm, allowing the grain to contribute to the film’s sense of actuality.

The discussion of visual aesthetics brought him to Cuban filmmaker Julio García Espinosa’s idea of “imperfect cinema,” which, for Kasaravalli, challenged the assumption that Western aesthetics should define cinematic perfection. “I don’t want to take your aesthetic sensibility as my sensibility,” he said, quoting Espinosa. Kasaravalli said each country and each region has its own aesthetic concerns. “We should make films according to that condition; we should never imitate the West. Why should I be perfect? I should be loyal to my own milieu.”

Asked whether parallel cinema belonged to a particular period, Kasaravalli disagreed, pointing to notable films being made across the country today. The larger problem, he said, was reaching audiences. Of streaming platforms, he said: “We all thought it is going to be a solution for all of us. But OTT [streaming] is exactly doing what the mainstream distribution network was doing earlier.”

In the audience Q&A, he addressed open endings and whether a filmmaker should provide answers. “A filmmaker who says that he knows answers for all the questions in life is telling a lie,” he said. “He is also as confused as you are. He only throws up options so that you can start thinking. There is nothing called closure… life is always open.”

As the session concluded, IFFI festival director Ashutosh Gowariker described Kasaravalli’s work as “textbooks,” saying he had returned to “Ghatashraddha” while developing a new film. He also singled out the filmmaker’s close-ups. “You let your actors simply be. You make the audience think and anticipate what the character is going through,” Gowariker said.

Shop the Mules Celebrities Can’t Get Enough Of

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Mules are making a comeback as the surprise hit of fall footwear, with celebrities wearing the minimalist silhouette on repeat in their everyday wardrobes. If summer was all about the wedge mule, this season flatter styles are taking center stage, with fans like Kendall Jenner and Hailey Bieber leading the charge. Meanwhile, ‘90s-inspired kitten-heel mules are also gaining traction among those who prefer a little extra height—a nod, perhaps, to the enduring Carolyn Bessette-Kennedy effect, further fueled by the TV series Love Story.

One brand celebrities continue to gravitate toward is The Row, whose refined flat designs are well suited for day-to-night wear. Bieber is a fan of the pared-back Penelope patent slippers, while Jenner favors the June mules, another take that fits right into her minimalist -leaning aesthetic. Elsewhere, Jennifer Lawrence makes a case for the chunkier Hudson slip-ons in brown suede, a comfortable option with a little more weight for walking.

Of course, there are plenty of alternatives beyond The Row. Lawrence has also been seen in a leopard-print pair by Ancient Greek Sandals on multiple occasions, adding a welcome dose of pattern to her modern boho looks. Julianne Moore, meanwhile, gave the trend a little lift in a pair of kitten heels by Bottega Veneta—a chic alternative to the season’s flatter styles. Whether you prefer a pared-back leather slipper, a statement-making print, or a touch of height, there’s a mule for just about every wardrobe.

Ahead, shop the season’s best mules, with styling inspiration straight from the wardrobes of your favorite celebrities.

Hailey Bieber is loyal to The Row’s Penelope mules, styling the carefree slippers with everything from lady jackets and jeans to a cobalt trench coat, black baby tee, and cropped kick-flare trousers, as she recently demonstrated on a night out in Los Angeles. Add a burgundy shoulder bag, and you get an easy day-to-night formula for transitional weather and beyond.

Easy, comfortable footwear is a staple for Jennifer Lawrence. As a New Yorker, she racks up plenty of miles on foot and bike, making The Row’s brown suede Hudson slides a practical choice, designed with a chunkier sole that can handle the steps. Style them as she did with an oversized navy crewneck sweater, a black T-shirt, and light-wash straight-leg jeans for effortless off-duty flair, then finish with a bold handbag for an unexpected pop of color.

Kendall Jenner is another die-hard The Row fan, her mule of choice being the June style. Keep your own wardrobe in a minimalist vein by borrowing her understated formula: a black bateau-neck top and navy slip skirt. (Yes, the two shades work together!) The combination feels instantly elevated with sleek mules, The Row’s camel Terrasse shoulder bag, silver hoop earrings, and Jenner’s favorite Chimi sunglasses.

Lawrence also has a soft spot for statement shoes, and this leopard-print pair from Ancient Greek Sandals brings a playful dose of pattern to her fall wardrobe. For a recent night out, she styled them with a sheer open-knit duster, white T-shirt, and floral Dries Van Noten skirt for an unexpected pattern clash that plays up her bohemian side.

Julianne Moore went for height in Bottega Veneta’s kitten-heel mules while promoting her new film, The Debut. The ’90s-inspired silhouette felt right at home alongside an inky-hued cardigan, white long-sleeve T-shirt, and the house’s statement skirt for a fashion-forward finish. A suede clutch and silver jewelry complete the look, adding just the right amount of polish.

5 Fall/Winter 2026 Wedding Guest Dress Trends You Can Still Shop

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The season of celebration is upon us. With winter weddings kicking into full swing and the Resort and Spring collection deliveries just around the corner, now is the moment to make a final play for Fall/Winter 2026’s most coveted runway looks. After all, self-expression is the unofficial theme du jour, with designers embracing personality-driven dressing through bold colors and lush fabrics; so much of what we saw lends itself perfectly to formalwear. In the wedding guest dress department, there’s no shortage of ways to make an entrance, from full-on glamour to elegant twists on tradition.

Use the season’s defining trends your starting point. Art Deco made a glamorous impression on the runways, with geometric shapes and ornate embellishments taking center stage. Its offshoots, like plush velvet and fringe detailing, feel especially apropos for winter weddings; as does red, a festive and bold colorway that appeared this season across a range of silhouettes from designers like Carolina Herrera and Tory Burch. And for a bit of drama, we’re loving all things dark romance—think, sheer lace, decadent ribboning, and an all-over inky palette.

Before a new season takes over the fashion conversation, give Fall/Winter one last moment in the spotlight—and find your next wedding look in the process. Scroll down to shop our favorite dresses straight from the runways.

Maximilian Davis at Ferragamo, Peter Copping at Lanvin and Daniella Kallmeyer each offered exquisite interpretations of the style that feel destined for the dance floor—and give you every reason to stay until the last song. With their Art Deco-inspired silhouettes and unabashed glamour, these shorter styles are made for celebrations that sit just shy of black tie. To keep the look on the right side of modern sophistication, remember: the trick is to channel the era, not recreate it. Resist the temptation to over-accessorize; a considered approach to jewelry will let the dress do all the talking.

This time of year, red is basically a neutral; accessorize with glitzy metallics for the more festive ceremonies on your calendar. From Carolina Herrera and Fendi to Tory Burch, designers offered a compelling range of silhouettes to suit every sensibility and dress code. The most spirited interpretation came courtesy of Duran Lantink for Jean Paul Gaultier, whose voluminous sleeves were ever so slightly tempered in the commercial version of the dress. Its ankle length is best-suited for a black-tie optional wedding (or anything that leans more thematic).

Romance took a darker turn this season. Erdem, Simone Rocha, The Attico, and Altuzarra explored their own interpretations through inky lace, sheer chiffon, high collars, and dramatic bows. The effect is alluring, a little subversive, and imbued with just the right amount of mystery for an after-hours wedding affair. Here, makeup plays a more important role—keep your lipstick understated, choosing a muted neutral over anything crimson to avoid veering into vamp territory.

Whether draped, ruched, or ruffled, velvet brings an inherent sense of luxury to the wedding circuit. Its versatility makes it a fitting choice for everything from intimate ceremonies to grand affairs; the silhouette and hemline, of course, will determine its dress-code credentials. When it comes to styling, take a cue from Pieter Mulier’s Alaïa, where velvet gowns were paired with barely-there shoes and luminous, glowing skin. Jewelry, if any, should be kept to a minimum: A single, discreet earring is all the embellishment you need when the dress is this rich.

If velvet brought the opulence this season, fringe supplied the theatrics. Leave any bohemian preconceptions at the door: This season, the embellishment took on a decidedly more sophisticated character. Gabriela Hearst, Ulla Johnson, Akris, and Altuzarra each offered their own interpretation, from fluid strands tracing the contours of sleek evening gowns to exuberant cascades that transformed minimalist silhouettes into something way more fun. These are dresses best-suited to cocktail receptions and formal RSVPs, where a little theatricality is always welcome.

Why Effie Trinket From The Hunger Games: Sunrise On The Reaping Looks So Familiar

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Effie Trinket, the character played by Elizabeth Banks in the original set of "Hunger Games" movies, is a larger-than-life, ridiculous, and flamboyant woman initially concerned with appearance above all else — even though her job is to beautify and briefly pamper impoverished children before they head to their deaths. It's hard to imagine this character, who appears in "The Hunger Games," "Catching Fire," and both parts of the adaptation of the book "Mockingjay," being played by anybody but Banks, yet the newest addition to the "Hunger Games" universe, the film version of "Sunrise on the Reaping," features a younger iteration of Effie. So, who brings her to life, and what role does Effie play in this prequel?

Suzanne Collins released the novel "Sunrise on the Reaping" in 2025, and before the book even hit shelves, a film adaptation (with the expanded title "The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping") was in the works. Ultimately, Joseph Zada was cast as a young Haymitch Abernathy, the character made famous by Woody Harrelson alongside Banks in the first four "Hunger Games" movies. A ton of huge names, including Kieran Culkin, Jesse Plemons, Glenn Close, and Ralph Fiennes, went on to join the movie's cast from there, with Elle Fanning being recruited to fill Banks' sky-high pastel shoes and play Effie as well.

We see Effie in "Sunrise on the Reaping" as a fledgling stylist desperate to prove herself ... and not only does she end up styling Haymitch and his fellow District 12 tributes when a senior stylist falls through, but she provides emotional support to Haymitch before and after he wins the Games. But where have you seen Fanning before? Plenty of places.

If the last name "Fanning" just looks familiar from the outset, that's because Elle Fanning and her older sister Dakota Fanning have been performing since they were kids. In fact, the younger Fanning played a younger version of her sister while making her film debut in "I Am Sam" in 2001.

From there, Elle Fanning showed up in family-friendly fare like "Daddy Day Care" and "Because of Winn-Dixie" (as well as considerably more adult films like "Babel"), but the fact of the matter is that Fanning essentially grew up on-screen. Both Fanning sisters navigated their journeys from child stars to adult actors in pretty incredible ways, but we're talking about Elle Fanning here, and her career does stand on its own. After playing younger roles in "Super 8," "We Bought a Zoo," and "Maleficent," she moved into more firmly adult parts in the films "The Neon Demon," "20th Century Women," "The Beguiled," and "Mary Shelley," just to name a few.

Without question, Fanning's most visible big-screen appearances aside from "The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping" have recently come in "A Complete Unknown," "Predator: Badlands," and "Sentimental Value." In "A Complete Unknown," she plays Sylvie Russo, the girlfriend to Timothée Chalamet's Bob Dylan and a character who's loosely based on Dylan's real girlfriend Suze Rotolo. In "Predator: Badlands," Fanning plays the protagonist Thia and her evil twin sister Tessa and was able to use her own odd physicality for the role. As for "Sentimental Value," Joachim Trier's gorgeous family drama earned Fanning her first Academy Award nomination for Best Supporting Actress at the 2026 ceremony.

Remarkably, Elle Fanning's small-screen career is just as robust as her big-screen one — and she's earned almost as much acclaim to boot. I personally remember seeing the younger Fanning as early as 2006 in the particularly depraved "Law & Order: Special Victims Unit" episode "Cage" (fans of that show know exactly which one I'm talking about), but 2020 marked her big TV break with "The Great," a truly great — pun intended! — historical drama that casts Fanning as Catherine the Great. In this deliberately anachronistic, deeply funny, and exceptionally bawdy series created by "The Favourite" screenwriter Tony McNamara, Fanning gets to play the future Empress of Russia alongside a deliciously clownish Nicholas Hoult as her power-hungry and utterly twisted husband Emperor Peter III ... and earned her first Emmy nomination for Outstanding Lead Actress in a Comedy Series for her efforts.

Sadly, "The Great" ended in 2023 after only three seasons, although Fanning's next TV project, the crime drama miniseries "The Girl from Plainville," was well-received. In 2026, though, Fanning earned overwhelming acclaim and another Emmy nod for her lead role in "Margo's Got Money Troubles," a collaboration between A24 and Apple TV that proved to be one of the very best shows of the year. As a young single mother who launches an OnlyFans career as a colorful, coquettish alien to support her young son, Fanning is extraordinary as Margo, and it's clear that this ambitious and incredibly talented actor will keep racking up credits and accolades for years to come.

"The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping" is currently in theaters.

Hello Sunshine’s Lauren Neustadter to Leave Reese Witherspoon’s Company at End of Year

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Lauren Neustadter, president of film and television at Reese Witherspoon‘s Hello Sunshine, is set to step down from the company at the end of the year, the collaborators announced on Friday. Her exit comes after Hello Sunshine let go of about 50% of its staff last month after Witherspoon and Mediawan partnered to buy the company back from Candle Media.

“Lauren’s taste, passion and leadership have been integral to Hello Sunshine’s film and television success,” Witherspoon said in a statement. “I’m so proud of what we accomplished together and grateful for her partnership and the lasting foundation she built around telling powerful, complex female-driven stories that resonate.” Related Stories 'Beast in Me' Season 2 Casts Riley Keough, Lorelei Olivia Mote, Aaron Paul, Taip Ceman, Matthew James Thomas Priyanka Chopra Jonas, Russell Crowe Sci-Fi Thriller 'Bluefly' Adds 'Beast' Star Daniel MacPherson (EXCLUSIVE)

Neustadter added in a statement, “Building Hello Sunshine alongside Reese and our incredible team has been the privilege of a lifetime. I’m deeply proud of all the shows and movies we’ve made, the doors we’ve opened for new voices, and the ways we have put women in the spotlight both in front of and behind the camera. I am grateful for every moment of it, and I will always be cheering for Hello Sunshine.”

Neustadter joined the company in 2017, and has served as executive producer on series including “The Morning Show,” “Big Little Lies,” “Little Fires Everywhere” and “The Last Thing He Told Me.” She oversaw adaptations of books from Reese’s Book Club including “Tiny Beautiful Things” and “Daisy Jones & the Six.” She also worked on feature films including “Where the Crawdads Sing” and TriStar’s upcoming “The Nightingale.”

Neustadter started her career as a film executive at Miramax and Twentieth Century Fox, then became an executive at FOX network, where she oversaw “Brooklyn Nine-Nine,” “The Mindy Project” and “Lethal Weapon.”

Hello Sunshine plans to focus on premium film and television and Reese’s Book Club, as well as expanding into new storytelling formats and distribution models.

3 Best Movies To Watch On Netflix This Weekend (November 21-22)

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Reminders of Him, one of the hit adaptations of Colleen Hoover novels, is the only film of that trio to be trending on Netflix nationwide. At the time of writing, Reminders of Him stands as the 7th most popular movie on Netflix in the United States. Last weekend's recommendations did not feature a single original film from the streamer, which I decided to change for our new options.

As for the only licensed film on our Netflix recommendations, that project happens to be one of the most popular movies of 2026, a true win for theaters. It is also one of the most engaging horror movies in recent history, birthing a couple of stars. These are the best movies to watch on Netflix this weekend:

Obsession is the only non-Netflix movie on the list, and it became a huge hit. With a budget of under $1 million and a box office total of $524.5 million, Obsession surpassed even the creative team's wildest expectations for the movie. All of that excitement is backed up by a solid concept with plenty of scary and unsettling moments, as well as a magnetic performance from Inde Navarrette.

Navarrette plays Nikki Freeman, who is changed by a supernatural event, as Michael Johnston's Bear makes a wish to a One Wish Willow, and it leads to Nikki becoming obsessed with him. That setup is perfect to give Navarrette a meaty role as Nikki slowly loses more and more of herself, only living to please Bear and going to extreme lengths to keep him only for her. Obsession is dark, topical, and comes as a breath of fresh air, birthing stars, with Navarrette cast as Rogue in Marvel's X-Men movie and Johnston starring in Brendan Fraser's The Mummy: Book of the Dead.

Staying in a similar dark tone to Obsession is the first of the Netflix Original movies on the list. Heartland stars Jessica Chastain as a former country singer on a mission. She comes back to Nashville to try to solve the mystery behind the sudden disappearance of her niece, Charlie Heart, a rising country star in her own right.

Heartland is a thriller with a sound lead performance from Chastain, who plays around with an intensity and swagger that elevate the overall story when it comes short. It also comes with a talented ensemble in the form of Garrett Hedlund (Tulsa King), Ross Lynch (Chilling Adventures of Sabrina), and more.

Finally, if you are looking for a delicious and fun romance to get lost in this weekend on Netflix, then the perfect call is the early holiday film Guarding Stars, which arrived on the streamer on November 18. Guarding Stars has two major actors in lead roles, with Supernatural's Jared Padalecki playing Jack Stapleton, an action movie star, and Gossip Girl's Leighton Meester as Hannah Brooks, the bodyguard hired to protect him.

Guarding Stars is the #1 movie on Netflix in the United States right now.

The characters' personalities could not be more different, but it is from their constant bickering and clashes that a romantic spark first manifests itself, and Padalecki has undeniable chemistry with Meester. Guarding Stars is based on Katherine Center's novel The Bodyguard. Guarding Stars should provide plenty of laughs and romance to set up Christmas season on Netflix this weekend.

‘Four Minus Three’ Takes Cairo Film Festival’s Golden Pyramid, While ‘Summer Meadow’ Wins Three Awards and ‘The Day of Wrath’ Nabs Two

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Austrian filmmaker Adrian Goiginger’s “Four Minus Three” emerged as the big winner at the 47th Cairo Intl. Film Festival, taking home three awards, including the Golden Pyramid for best film, as the festival wrapped its 10-day run in the Egyptian capital on Friday.

The Austrian-German drama, which follows a professional clown struggling to find hope after losing her family in a car accident, also earned best actress for Valerie Pachner and best supporting actor for Robert Stadlober. Franco-Moroccan filmmaker Nabil Ayouch (“Everybody Loves Touda”) headed the international competition jury. Related Stories 'Beast in Me' Season 2 Casts Riley Keough, Lorelei Olivia Mote, Aaron Paul, Taip Ceman, Matthew James Thomas Priyanka Chopra Jonas, Russell Crowe Sci-Fi Thriller 'Bluefly' Adds 'Beast' Star Daniel MacPherson (EXCLUSIVE)

French filmmaker Rudi Rosenberg took the Silver Pyramid for best director for “Words of Love,” while Egyptian filmmaker Ashgan El-Hamus received the Bronze Pyramid special jury prize for “Al Baraneya.” Moroccan filmmaker Laïla Marrakchi and screenwriter Delphine Agut won best screenplay for “Strawberries.” The Henry Barakat Award for best artistic contribution went to cinematographer Marius Panduru and production designer Hendrik Van Kets for the Belgian-Dutch-German co-production “Heysel 85,” which revisits the deadly 1985 Heysel Stadium disaster in Brussels. The film, directed by Teodora Ana Mihai, also won the FIPRESCI critics’ prize.

In the Horizons of Arab Cinema competition, Rania Rafei’s “The Day of Wrath: Tales from Tripoli,” a documentary tracing decades of political uprisings in Lebanon’s second-largest city, won best Arab film and the festival’s best documentary award.

Sara Shazli’s “Your Daughter,” a personal documentary exploring the Egyptian filmmaker’s complicated relationship with her mother, producer and filmmaker Marianne Khoury, received the Horizons special jury prize.

Naël Khleifi and Lisa Debauche’s “Summer Meadow,” which follows a Lebanese apprentice shepherdess living in isolation in the French Alps, collected three prizes, including the $10,000 best Arab feature film award and a shared creative achievement prize. Manal Issa also won best acting performance for the film, after previously winning the Pardo for Best Performance in Locarno’s Cineasti del Presente competition for the same role. Mohamed Zineddaine’s “Until Dawn” shared the creative achievement award.

In the Critics’ Week section, Lorenzo Ferro and Lucas A. Vignale’s “The River Train,” an Argentine drama about a nine-year-old folk dancer who dreams of escaping his rural village for Buenos Aires, won best film. Azamat Shargyn’s “Before the Sun Rises in the West” received the special jury prize, while Ishtiyak Ahmad Zihad’s “The Blind Girl and an Elephant” earned a special mention.

In the short film competition, Lenti Liang’s “Our Secrets” won best short film, while Pranav Bhasin’s “We Were Here” received the special jury prize. Sabrine Khoury’s “A Tenderness” earned a special mention.

The Youssef Cherif Rizkallah Audience Award went to Egyptian director Ahmad Abdalla’s “BerleeN_,” an ensemble drama following three Arab residents of the German capital whose lives intersect over the course of a single day.

The closing ceremony at the Cairo Opera House also saw veteran Egyptian cinematographer Said Shimi receive the Golden Pyramid Lifetime Achievement Award.

Marking 50 years since its founding in 1976, this year’s festival presented 145 films from 64 countries, including 17 world premieres, five international premieres and 87 MENA premieres. The event drew 53,000 ticket sales, according to festival president Hussein Fahmy, up from approximately 45,000 last year.

The program included screenings of restored Egyptian classics, the new Cairo Kids section and masterclasses with filmmakers Matteo Garrone and international competition jury president Nabil Ayouch, who were both honored with Golden Pyramid Lifetime Achievement Awards at the festival’s opening ceremony.

The festival’s eighth Cairo Industry Days brought together filmmakers, producers and industry professionals for workshops, pitching sessions and discussions. Its 12th Cairo Film Connection featured 17 projects from 12 countries that competed for 53 awards with a combined value of $410,000, including cash grants, in-kind services and invitations to international industry platforms.

E.J. Carroll Dies: ‘The Deuce,’ ‘Boardwalk Empire,’ ‘Law & Order’ Actor Was 66

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

E.J. Carroll, a veteran character actor whose credits include The Deuce, Boardwalk Empire, Blue Bloods three Law & Order series and Grand Theft Auto V, died November 19. He was 66. His rep Jon Mason of Mason-Lewis Entertainment said Carroll died suddenly while en route to a voice-over booking, but he did not provide a cause of death.

Born on May 21, 1960, Carroll appeared in features, television, short films and video games during a nearly three-decade screen career. His first screen credit was playing a cop in a 1998 episode of NBC’s Law & Order, and he would appear as other characters in three other episodes through 2004. He also had roles in two early-2000s episodes of its juggernaut spinoff Law & Order: SVU and two more in Law & Order: Criminal Intent that decade.

He would appear in a number of movies during the later 2000s and into the 2010s including Darren Aronfsky’s acclaimed 2009 drama The Wrestler, along with The Cake Eaters, The Capers, The Winning Season, The Hungry Ghosts, A Fish Story and the Robert De Niro-Kate Beckinsale pic Everything’s Fine, among others.

Carroll played Sheriff Victor Sickles in two 2012 episodes of HBO’s lauded period crime drama Boardwalk Empire, from Tenrence Winter and MArtin Scorsese and starring Steve Buscemi, appeared in episode of FX’s Glenn Close-Rose Byrne legal thriller Damages that same year. He went on to guest on NBC’s The Blacklist, CBS’ Blue Bloods, HBO’s High Maintenance, FX’s Pose and Netflix’s Marvel series The Punisher.

He landed perhaps his most recognizable role in 2017, recurring on the HBO’ ‘s The Deuce as Fat Mooney, who ran a New York City sex shop owned by Frankie Martino (James Franco). He appeared in a half-dozen episodes of the series about the rise of the porn industry in the 1970s and ’80s.

Most recently, Carroll filmed a role in A Delicate Arrangement, the upcoming film written and produced by Jayce Bartok and directed by Nick Sandow. It stars Carla Gugino as Vivien Leigh in the true story of two-time Oscar winning actress’ relationship with a Philadelphia florist (Matthew Modine). David Hyde Pierce also stars in the film, which is in post-production.

“E.J. Carroll was a consummate actor known and loved for his intense commitment to the characters he played,” said who noted that Carroll was passionate about A Delicate Arrangement and helped ensure the project would be completed. “There isn’t an actor in New York that hadn’t worked with or crossed paths with E.J., known for stealing scenes alongside Robert De Niro, Mickey Rourke, Bruce Dern and most recently Jalen Brunson and Quinta Brunson. He will be greatly missed by the acting community which he lived for.”

RELATED: Celebrities Who’ve Voiced Characters In ‘Grand Theft Auto’

Carroll also voiced a character in the blockbuster 2013 video game Grand Theft Auto and played Dr. Scott in The Rocky Horror Tribute Show, a concert version of The Rocky Horror Show that played one night only in 2007 as a benefit for cancer charities Acting for a Cure/Komen for the Cure.

Information about survivors or a memorial service was not immediately available.

Doctor Doom’s Scrapped MCU Variant Unveiled In New Avengers: Doomsday Release

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

A scrapped version of Doctor Doom has surfaced online ahead of Avengers: Doomsday's release.

Robert Downey Jr. is set to make his MCU return as Victor von Doom in Avengers: Doomsday. This new role is a complete 180 for the actor as he goes from the heroic Tony Stark, also known as Iron Man, to the infamous villain Doctor Doom. His casting was announced at San Diego Comic-Con in 2024, exciting fans of the actor who are excited to see him bring this classic comic book villain to life on the big screen.

A new image of Doctor Doom has emerged ahead of Avengers: Doomsday, offering Marvel Cinematic Universe fans a look at an alternate costume that was reportedly dropped. Although Downey Jr.'s iteration of the villain has already appeared in promo material for the highly anticipated crossover, one of the original designs offers a completely different take on the character.

A new series of images features an upcoming Lord Doom collectible figure, which is based on concept art created for Avengers: Doomsday. Instead of depicting the villain's official design in the upcoming superhero film, the figure was inspired by an alternative variant that really hones in on his role as the ruler of Latveria.

The collectible features Doom wearing a dark green cloak with his iconic hood. The costume also has gold buttons, chains, and a huge belt. His jacket includes a Latverian logo. However, despite the different outfit, the action figure of the character is still wearing his infamous metallic mask.

Although the collectible is officially associated with Avengers: Doomsday, its appearance should not be seen as Doctor Doom's final costume in the movie. The action figure is specifically based on concept art instead of what the character will actually look like in the upcoming film.

This is not the first time concept art of Lord Doom has appeared in merchandise for Avengers: Doomsday. Hasbro previously released a Marvel Legends action figure featuring a similar design, as well as Funko! Pop's version of the character. Both toys were also described as being inspired by concept art from the film.

Directed by Anthony and Joe Russo, Avengers: Doomsday brings together a star-studded lineup of some of Marvel's most iconic actors, even from films that predate the MCU, such as the original X-Men. The massive ensemble cast includes Patrick Stewart (Professor X), Ian McKellen (Magneto), James Marsden (Cyclops), Rebecca Romijn (Mystique), Kelsey Grammer (Beast), Alan Cumming (Nightcrawler), Chris Evans (Steve Rogers), Hayley Atwell (Peggy Carter), Chris Hemsworth (Thor), Anthony Mackie (Sam Wilson), Paul Rudd (Ant-Man), Simu Liu (Shang-Chi), Tom Hiddleston (Loki), Winston Duke (M'Baku), and more.

Avengers: Doomsday will be released in theaters on December 18, 2026.
