Eminem Takes a Trip Down Memory Lane for ‘The Slim Shady Show’-Inspired Episode of Spotify’s ‘Billions Club’

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Em gets quizzed about his classic hits by his alter ego.

Eminem joined forces with Spotify to celebrate the Detroit legend becoming part of the streaming giant’s Billions Club with an episode from the popular series, which arrived on Wednesday (Nov. 18).

In typical Em fashion, he put his own creative spin on the visual, drawing inspiration from his animated The Slim Shady Show from 2000.

In the episode, Eminem appears at the Spotify office surrounded by Billions Club accolades, and then he’s fittingly served a dish of his mom’s spaghetti in the “Lose Yourself” plaque.

The “Not Afraid” rapper gets annoyed when sauce spills on his new Air Jordan 4s, and then an old friend taking on his Slim Shady persona greets him from the center of the pasta dish.

Em is then pulled into an animated world and takes a trip down memory lane while getting quizzed on his classic hits that have joined the Billions Club over the years, including “Not Afraid,” “Stan,” “Lose Yourself,” “Love the Way You Lie” with Rihanna, “Godzilla” featuring Juice WRLD, “Mockingbird,” “Superman,” “Till I Collapse,” “Rap God” and Akon’s “Smack That.”

The episode closes with Em returning to the real world and threatening to kill his Slim Shady alter ego, who’s hiding out in the spaghetti, a callback to his 2024 album, The Death of Slim Shady (Coup de Grâce), which arrived in July 2024 and topped the Billboard 200.

Eminem has reached the summit of the Billboard Hot 100 on five separate occasions in his decorated career. He reached No. 1 with both of his Rihanna collaborations “Love the Way You Lie” and “The Monster,” as well as “Not Afraid,” “Lose Yourself” and “Crack a Bottle,” the last track with his close friends Dr. Dre and 50 Cent.

50 actually revealed in October that Em is working on a new album, which caused the Detroit rapper to pull out of contributing to the Street Fighter soundtrack. Fif told the Associated Press at the film’s premiere: “Em, he put a couple pieces together for it and decided because he got closer to his album, he kinda fell back on that because he’s preparing his presentation for the next album.”

Frank Reich: I'm the Jets' offensive coordinator, our inability to score starts with me

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Jets haven’t scored a touchdown since the third quarter of their Week 8 loss to the Raiders. It’s now Week 11, and Jets offensive coordinator Frank Reich knows it’s past time for him to find a way to get his team into the end zone.

Reich said today that he believes the Jets have enough talent on offense to produce, and he and the coaching staff need to make it happen.

“I’m the offensive coordinator, it starts with me,” Reich said. “I, and we as a staff, have to put the players in position to do what they do. We’ve got a very talented group, so we need to do a better job coaching.”

Over the last nine quarters of play, the Jets have nine Jason Sanders field goals, and no other points. Reich is well aware that three points a quarter isn’t going to win many games.

“Obviously, players need to do their part and execute and make plays,” Reich said. “For all of us, it’s a collective effort. We know we need to put more points on the board, whether it’s in the red zone or whether it’s further out, we still want to be explosive. We need to get back on track there. It’s been nine quarters. Believe me, I feel every play of that nine quarters.”

12 Best Acting Performances In Steven Spielberg Movies, Ranked

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Steven Spielberg is one of, if not the most iconic directors working today. The filmmaker’s first project dates all the way back to 1971 with the release of Duel on ABC (a direct to television film), and he has since gone on to helm countless other titles. In total, Spielberg has directed a whopping 36 projects, and is still active to this day, with the release of Disclosure Day coming earlier this year.

Many of the classic films from the last forty years can be attributed to Spielberg. The director helmed 1993s Jurassic Park, for example, and he also did the first four Indiana Jones films, along with Schindler’s List, Saving Private Ryan, Jaws, and many, many more. Impressively, Spielberg has switched genres frequently in his career, never staying in one area for too long (his two most recent projects, for example, are The Fablemans and Disclosure Day, and both are vastly different).

Spielberg isn’t just known for his pure directing talents, however. He’s also made a reputation for himself as one of the best “actor’s directors” working today; that is, having the ability to work with all kinds of unique performers and get the best out of them each and every single time.

There’s plenty of evidence to back up that claim, too. Just as some of the best movies of the last forty years have come from Spielberg, some of the best performances from actors have come in these projects as well. And while the actors naturally deserve great credit for these turns, Spielberg should also get his plaudits for being the mastermind behind these performances.

One of Spielberg’s earlier films was the iconic Jaws. Everyone knows the story of this one — a giant shark circles the waters of a coastal town, with a small group of townsfolk warning others of the dangers, but are up against a mayor who is unwilling to shut down the beaches.

Interestingly, Spielberg reportedly assembled a small group of A-list actors that he wanted to appear in the film. At the same time, the director was also hesitant to choose anyone too well known, as he didn’t want their fame to overshadow the movie. As such, the likes of Robert Redford, Paul Newman, and Steve McQueen, all names Spielberg had in mind, were not in Jaws.

One name that was in Jaws, however, is Robert Shaw. The English actor was well known at the time, both in the United Kingdom and the United States (although he was especially famous in the former, his homeland). Before Jaws, Shaw had appeared in the likes of From Russia With Love, The Taking of Pelham One Two Three, and A Man for All Seasons (for which he received an Academy Award nomination).

In Jaws, Shaw plays Quint, a grizzled and hardened former soldier who survived a massive shipwreck during World War II. He, very famously, has a strong hatred for sharks, but along with that, he also carries extensive knowledge of these animals as well. Shaw himself is arguably the standout performer of the film, especially because of his speech on the USS Indianapolis, in which he says that sharks have “lifeless eyes, black eyes, like a doll’s eyes.”

Another standout performer from Jaws is the film’s lead hero, Roy Schneider, who plays Chief Martin Brody. Like Shaw, Jaws ended up being the biggest role of Schneider’s career (and one he would reprise in future sequels, including Jaws II).

Ironically, Chief Brody has a deep-rooted fear of the water, which makes his quest for the shark even more intense. The audience can truly feel Brody’s anxiety and stress through Schneider’s performance, which, in turn, makes the movie feel more lived in and real. At the same time, Schneider does brilliantly to ensure his character isn’t defined by fear, making this one of the very best performances in any Steven Spielberg movie.

Just four years after Jaws, Steven Spielberg launched what ended up being another one of his most classic projects. Spielberg developed the character of Indiana Jones alongside George Lucas and Lawrence Kasdan, and then helmed Jones’ first movie, Raiders of the Lost Ark.

Harrison Ford’s first performance as Indiana Jones remains just as iconic as it was nearly fifty years ago. Ford exudes confidence and swagger, making Indiana Jones one of the very best heroes in movie history. After the first Star Wars movie in 1977, it would have been hard to predict that Ford would come up with another character just as iconic, if not more so than Han Solo, but he does just that in Raiders of the Lost Ark (thanks, in part, to Steven Spielberg’s stellar direction).

As far as intimate projects go, arguably no film in recent history has been as meaningful to its director as The Fablemans. The 2022 film serves as Spielberg’s very own origin story, showing his upbringing and how he became interested in filmmaking in the first place. His parents play big roles in the film, especially his mother, who’s played by Michelle Williams, in what is one of the finest performances of her entire career.

Williams has given many memorable turns over the years, but The Fablemans truly stands out. Williams is truly outstanding in this film, playing a character who is so conflicted (and someone that Spielberg himself clearly has complicated feelings about). It’s far from the easiest role to play, especially when factoring in that this is her director’s mother, but Williams deserves great credit.

Arguably the main attraction of Jurassic Park as a film are the dinosaurs. The special effects were exceptional in 1993 and remain so to this day, and along with Spielberg’s direction, help to make these prehistoric creatures feel truly alive. Yet, at the same time, the human characters are just as impressive in Jurassic Park, particularly Jeff Goldblum’s Dr. Ian Malcom.

Malcom is a mathematician who excels in chaos theory, and is highly skeptical about John Hammond’s Earth-shattering invention right from the get-go. Goldblum plays Malcom tremendously, making him a witty character that, at the same time, also demands to be taken seriously. Goldblum helps tie the entire movie together, in fact, making this another top-tier performance in a Steven Spielberg film.

Spielberg incredibly made not one, but two incredible films in 1993. One was the aforementioned Jurassic Park, while the other was a vastly different story — Schindler’s List. The film told the true story of a German industrialist named Oskar Schindler who put his life on the line to save thousands of Jewish people from the Holocaust.

Liam Neeson plays Schindler in what is likely the finest performance of his entire career. There’s so much emotion entrenched in Schindler’s List, and that doesn’t just come from the script or Spielberg’s direction (both of which are excellent). Neeson is extraordinary here, proving himself more than capable of delivering an iconic dramatic performance.

Another iconic 1990s film from Spielberg is Saving Private Ryan, which focuses on a mission to save Private James Francis Ryan (Matt Damon) after his three brothers were killed during the Battle of Normandy. Ryan is the core subject of the film, but Tom Hanks’ Captain John Miller is the one leading the mission.

Hanks is often labeled as one of the best actors to ever live, and Saving Private Ryan is often cited as evidence for this. It’s for good reason, too, as Hanks is this film’s standout. Despite Hanks’ immense fame, it’s easy to forget that he’s there at all, and instead, audiences are likely to simply see Captain Miller, which makes Saving Private Ryan feel all the more realistic.

Arguably the most divisive entry on this list, Tom Cruise received mixed responses to his performance as the leading protagonist in Spielberg’s War of the Worlds remake. The film itself received mixed reception, too, but Cruise does deserve a lot of credit for his performance here. Cruise plays a single dad incredibly well, and embodies what it feels like to be a parent during moments of danger.

One of the very best movies that Steven Spielberg ever made also features Tom Hanks - Catch Me if You Can. The film, which centers on Leonardo DiCaprio’s Frank Abagnale Jr., is actually a true story that you have to see to believe. DiCaprio’s Abagnale Jr. is eccentric, coming up with lies on the spot. As a result, he lives several different lives throughout the film, all with the FBI hot on his trail.

Hanks is, predictably, excellent as the FBI agent leading the charge. Yet, he’s outshone by DiCaprio, only in his early 20s at this time. And despite the fact that this was relatively early on in DiCaprio’s career, it remains one of his very best, and one of the very best performances in any Steven Spielberg film.

Whoopi Goldberg had her breakout role in Steven Spielberg’s 1985 adaption of The Color Purple. It’s no wonder that Goldberg became a star after, too, as she steals the film, vastly improving the story and making it more than worth the watch. A huge reason for this is the pain that Goldberg embodies throughout, making her character feel like a truly three-dimensional and real human being.

‘Landman’ Star’s New Racing Thriller Officially Unleashes Star-Studded Trailer

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Best known for his role as Terry in the Ocean’s franchise, Andy Garcia has been around as a prominent Hollywood actor for over 20 years. In the last few years, Garcia has settled into a cozy role in Landman, the blockbuster new series from Yellowstone creator Taylor Sheridan. After featuring in a guest role in the first season, Garcia stepped into a much larger role in Season 2 that’s expected to continue into Season 3, especially now that Tommy (played by Billy Bob Thornton) has started his own oil company. Earlier this year, Garcia also had a small role in the Kevin Hart-led Netflix comedy, 72 Hours, which began streaming on the platform at the end of July. However, he’s set to return to the big screen early next year for a new film that’s bringing out all the stars.

Andy Garcia is among the names attached to star in Maserati: The Brothers, the new film coming to theaters on January 29, 2027. Michele Morrone (The Housemaid), Vincent Piazza (Jersey Boys), Jessica Alba (Fantastic Four), Al Pacino (The Godfather), and Anthony Hopkins (The Silence of the Lambs) have also been tapped to star in the film, which was written by Amanda Moresco and directed by Bobby Moresco. This morning, Magenta Light Studios dropped the first official trailer for Maserati: The Brothers, confirming that the film will not be pushed back from its January 29, 2027, release date. Salvatore Esposito and Gina La Piana also star in the film, with Andrea Lervolino, Gary Michael Walters, Bob Yari, Danielle Maloni, Luca Matrundola, and a host of others serving as producers.

The show that claimed the most of your answers is the world you were built for. If two tied, both are shown — you're complicated enough to straddle two Sheridan universes.

You are a Dutton — or you might as well be. You understand that some things are worth protecting at any cost, and that the modern world's indifference to history, to land, to legacy, is not something you're willing to accept quietly. You lead from the front, you carry your family's weight without complaint, and when someone threatens what's yours, you don't escalate — you finish it. You're not cruel. But you are absolute. In Yellowstone's world, that combination of ferocity and loyalty doesn't make you a villain. It makes you the only thing standing between everything that matters and everyone who wants to take it.

You thrive in the chaos of high-stakes negotiation, where the money is enormous, the margins are thin, and the wrong word in the wrong room can cost everyone everything. You're a fixer — the person called when a situation is already on fire and needs someone with the nerve to walk into it. West Texas oil country rewards exactly what you are: sharp, adaptable, unsentimental, and absolutely clear-eyed about what people want and what they'll do to get it. You're not naive enough to think this world is fair. You're smart enough to be the one deciding who it's fair to.

You are a Dwight Manfredi — someone who has served their time, paid their dues, and arrived somewhere unexpected with nothing but their reputation and their wits. You adapt without losing yourself. You build loyalty through respect rather than fear, though you're not above reminding people that the two aren't mutually exclusive. Tulsa King is for people who are still standing when everyone assumed they'd be finished — who find, in an unfamiliar place, that they're more capable than the world gave them credit for. You don't need a throne. You build one, wherever you happen to land.

You carry the weight of a system that is broken by design, and you do it anyway — because someone has to, and because you're the only one positioned to do it without the whole thing collapsing. Mike McLusky's world is for people who are comfortable operating where there are no good options, only less catastrophic ones. You speak every language: law enforcement, criminal, political, human. That fluency makes you invaluable and it makes you a target. You've made your peace with both. Mayor of Kingstown belongs to people who understand that keeping the peace is not the same as being at peace — and who do the job regardless.

The official logline for Maserati: The Brothers reads as follows: “Chronicles the Maserati family's journey, from its founding in 1914 to becoming a renowned Italian luxury car manufacturer, alongside rivals Ferrari and Lamborghini.” After the success of F1 last year, it’s safe to say that the demand for more racing movies, especially ones about great racing families like Maserati, is higher than ever. Back in 2019, Matt Damon and Christian Bale teamed up for the epic racing movie, Ford v Ferrari, which is a must-watch for fans who can’t wait for the new Maserati movie. Between Ford v Ferrari and F1, Director Michael Mann teamed up with Adam Driver for Ferrari, which is now streaming on Netflix.

Check out the new trailer for Maserati: The Brothers and stay tuned to Collider for more updates and coverage of the film.

Here Are All the Surprise Star Cameos in Taylor Swift’s 2026 CMA Awards Performance

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

From host Lainey Wilson to Sugarland and beyond, country stars took part in Swift's "I Knew It, I Knew You" and "Our Song" set.

Man, it’s been awhile – 13 years to be exact – since Taylor Swift last performed at the CMA Awards, but when she returned to take the stage at the Bridgestone Arena in Nashville on Wednesday (Nov. 18), she did it in style and made sure it sparkled not only thanks to her sequined outfit, but with literal stars – country music stars, that is.

The whimsical performance – a mashup of her Toy Story 5 hit (which has topped the Billboard Hot 100 for two weeks so far) “I Knew It, I Knew You” and “Our Song” (No. 16 peak) from her self-titled 2006 debut album – kicked off backstage in her dressing room. Eagle-eyed Swifties likely noticed that quite a few of her debut-era outfits were strategically placed around the space. But look a little closer, and viewers might see that she had a very small but special – albeit not breathing – guest in the room: Jessie the doll from the Toy Story franchise.

That was just the first of numerous surprise sightings throughout the performance, which included several nods to the film series, including the backup dancers who were an homage to Woody, the blue sky and fluffy white clouds on the front of her guitar that harken back to the wallpaper in Andy’s room, the tree on stage that brought viewers back to Tire Swing Hill, and of course, the 14-time Grammy winner’s glittery, Jessie-inspired cowgirl outfit.

And as Swift left her dressing room and moved through the backstage area as she effortlessly sang “I Knew It, I Knew You,” country greats such as Little Big Town, Brooks & Dunn, host Lainey Wilson, Eric Church and “Whispering Bill” Anderson all greeted her on the way, with Keith Urban waiting on the Toy Story-inspired stage to perform alongside her.

Keep scrolling to see all of the country music stars who joined Taylor Swift for her first CMA Awards performance in more than a decade.

Take a closer look and you’ll see that the Jessie doll from the Toy Story franchise is sitting on the couch between some of the pop superstar’s outfits from her debut era.

Swift kept Paisley busy, as the “Whiskey Lullaby” crooner donned some rubber gloves to polish her CMA Awards trophies.

Cheese! The pop superstar struck a pose with Little Big Town for a quick snap.

Sugarland made a short and sweet appearance.

The singer helped move Brooks & Dunn’s card game along as she made her way through the backstage area during her set.

Swift plopped Kix Brooks’ burgundy cowboy hat on Lainey Wilson’s head, while the CMA Awards host handed the singer a pair of sunglasses.

Swift stopped for a quick and cool fist bump with Eric Church.

“Whispering Bill” was all smiles as he handed the superstar artist her sparkling Toy Story-themed guitar.

Urban was the only country artist to join Swift for the onstage part of her performance.

Man Of Tomorrow’s Superman Improvements Teased By David Corenswet Amid DCU Concerns

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Ryan Northrup is a Senior Staff Writer on ScreenRant's Movie & TV News team, where he has authored nearly 5,000 articles, including features and reviews. A graduate of McMaster University, Ryan began his career in public relations before attending Vancouver Film School. There, he built lasting industry connections and optioned a feature film screenplay.

Expect some Superman upgrades in Man of Tomorrow.

Following his introduction as Superman in James Gunn's movie of the same name last year, David Corenswet is set to return as the DC hero next summer for an anticipated sequel. Man of Tomorrow, which arrives following a shakeup at DC in which Gunn and Peter Safran are to step down from their executive leadership roles, will see Superman teaming up with Nicholas Hoult's Lex Luthor to face a new threat.

As the future of DC remains up in the air, Corenswet dropped by The Rich Eisen Show to discuss his time as Superman and what's to come in Man of Tomorrow. Corenswet comments on what it was like filming the original movie and all the firsts that came with that experience, not just in terms of physically transforming himself, but also in terms of becoming comfortable in the suit. He explains:

I mean, looking back, there were a lot of great challenges that came along with it: gaining the weight for the first time in my life, the wire work, the stunt work was a new thing for me. That was wonderful and challenging. I think back to what Christopher Reeve said about his thoughts going into shooting the original Superman: The Movie which was… Ultimately, it’s just you out there in the tights and nobody’s coming to save you.

And so sometimes the biggest challenge was just standing there for a scene in the tights and the trunks and somehow finding a way to not feel silly and not try too hard to make up for it in some way, not act too tough or shy away from what it is. I think it’s one of the great things about the character is that he’s proud to be the big red and blue and he’ll just stand there in his trunks and maybe you’ll laugh at him, but he’ll be happy to have made you laugh.

While addressing how he maintains his confidence while acting in tights next to his costars, Corenswet touches on Lex's advanced armor suit set to feature in the Superman sequel. The actor then jokingly asks why his character doesn't get his own armored suit:

It was really nice anytime I would walk up to somebody and they’d go like, “Oh my god, it’s Superman.” I go, “Okay, I’m going to take that with me for the next few minutes and hopefully that’ll keep my confidence up just long enough to get through the next scene.” Especially when you’re standing across from Rachel Brosnahan, who’s dressed all cool, and she looks so beautiful, and she’s so sharp, and Nicholas Hoult, and his cool tailored suits, and then his war suit in this movie we just shot. He’s got his big green war suit. Like, why can’t I get some armor?

Though Corenswet is careful not to reveal too much about Man of Tomorrow, he does tease that the film features some exciting flying and fight sequences. Speaking seemingly of the stunt and visual effects teams, Corenswet says that "they really up their game" from the first movie. Check out his comment below:

Love the interview scene with Rachel. That was such a fun scene to shoot. It was the first scene that we shot. It was two days and it was like shooting like a 1950s Cary Grant movie. Just the dialogue, quippy, and funny, and just about these characters. There were so many great action pieces. Actually, in this movie, in the next Superman movie that we finished this summer, there are some incredible new flying and fights. They really up their game there. So, in some ways, those are the ones I’m really excited for people to see those, ’cause those were a lot of fun to shoot and I think will end up being a lot of fun to do.

In addition to Corenswet, Brosnahan, and Hoult, Man of Tomorrow is set to feature the return of Skyler Gisondo as Jimmy Olsen, Wendell Pierce as Perry White, Isabela Merced as Hawkgirl, Frank Grillo as Rick Flag Sr., Edi Gathegi as Mr. Terrific, Mikaela Hoover as Cat Grant, and María Gabriela de Faría as the Engineer, among others.

The Superman follow-up will also feature a host of newcomers, including Lars Eidinger as Brainiac, Aaron Pierre as Green Lantern, Milly Alcock as Supergirl, Xolo Maridueña as Blue Beetle, and Matthew Lillard in an undisclosed role. Man of Tomorrow's upgraded flying and fighting scenes, then, will clearly accompany a much more expansive and star-studded cast.

Of course, the recent leadership shakeup at DC Studios has cast a shadow over Man of Tomorrow and has raised questions about the current iteration of DC heroes. After becoming co-CEOs of DC Studios in 2022, Gunn and Safran are confirmed to be stepping down from their roles effective December 1, 2026.

The development follows Paramount Skydance's acquisition of Warner Bros. Discovery on October 6, with the resulting company simply called Skydance. Jeremy Latcham is stepping in as president and chief creative officer, replacing Gunn and Safran.

Man of Tomorrow will follow a mixed series of releases for DC. Superman was a modest hit, grossing $618 million and earning strong reviews. Supergirl (2026), however, was a box office bomb this summer. Most recently, Clayface, which hit theaters on October 23, earned positive reviews and became a modest box office success. Gunn and Safran were also broadly successful on the TV front, with Peacemaker, Creature Commandos, and Lanterns all met with warm receptions.

Though many questions remain about Man of Tomorrow and the future of DC more broadly, audiences can evidently expect some exciting upgrades to Superman's flying and fighting scenes. With the sequel potentially serving as Corenswet's last outing as the DC hero, the movie is certainly shaping up to end things on a high note.

Christopher Nolan Explains Why Fans Will Never See an Odyssey Director’s Cut

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Don’t expect to see any deleted scenes in the epic’s home release

With a runtime of just under three hours, Christopher Nolan's The Odyssey doesn't necessarily need to be any longer, even as an adaptation of Homer's epic poem. Although it isn't Nolan's longest film, fans are always clamoring for more from the prolific director. With The Odyssey's release on home media​​​​​​​, there was some hope that a director's cut or deleted scenes would surface. Nolan has shut that idea down, going so far as to say people will likely never see director's cuts of his films.

Nolan has been pretty excited about the home release of The Odyssey. While the director is known for shooting in IMAX and encouraging audiences to see his movies on the biggest screens possible, he also knows home viewing has a place in movie culture. Speaking to Polygon, Nolan said that at home the viewer is able to control the timeline and look at details, specifically saying that "it's really about the performances." He also supports talking over the movie​​​​​​​ in the comfort of your home.

Still, home releases often come with bonuses such as deleted scenes. Nolan told The Independent, however, that isn't his style. “I don’t really believe in director’s cuts. I want to stand by the film that goes out,”​​​​​​​ the director said. He added that, in the case of The Odyssey, "there are a lot of deleted scenes up in the wastebasket that no one will ever see." Nolan stresses​​​​​​d that there aren't actually too many scenes that didn't make the final cut because shooting on film requires planning. "I tend to try and weed things out on paper because it’s crazy expensive to shoot things that aren’t going to be in the film." He admitted that this has led to a lack of bonuses on home releases, "which always disappoints the DVD crowd."

For those watching the movie for the first time or the tenth time​​​​​​​, Nolan does have some opinions on how to best emulate the movie theater experience. Specifically, he suggests people get a projector. "You can get a relatively low-cost, pretty high-quality projector, darken your living room, maybe introduce some popcorn into the equation or whatever," he told Polygon. "Have a mini movie theater and do it on your own timeline. That's tremendous fun."

Over the course of his career, Nolan said that he's been impressed by how much the home-viewing experience has improved thanks to technology like better projection technology​​​​​​​. Because of that, he told Polygon that a film like The Odyssey carries its cinematic excitement and spectacle to people's living rooms. "Even if people are seeing it for the first time, I think the film sort of carries that with it."

Stephen Colbert Signs With UTA

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Multiple Emmy Award winner Stephen Colbert and his production company Spartina Industries have signed with United Talent Agency for representation across film, TV, publishing, and more.

Colbert will continue to be managed by James ‘Baby Doll’ Dixon of DixonTalent, whom he has worked with for over 20 years.

The long-time late-night host, writer, and executive producer recently concluded his top-rated CBS series The Late Show with Stephen Colbert, which ran for 11 seasons. The series finale reached 6.74M live + same-day viewers, per preliminary panel-only data from Nielsen, becoming the most-watched weeknight episode ever, as Deadline reported in May. The series-high ratings were reached with a little help from his friends Paul McCartney, the Strike Force Five: Jimmy Kimmel, Jimmy Fallon, John Oliver and Seth Meyers, Jon Stewart, Andy Cohen, as well as Bryan Cranston, Paul Rudd, Tim Meadows, Elijah Woods and Ryan Reynolds. Related Stories News Rola Bauer Takes On Creative Director Role At Monte-Carlo Festival As Talent Agents & Producers Join Advisory Board Agencies 'Call Her Daddy's Alex Cooper Officially Moves To CAA After Leaving UTA

Prior to that, he hosted, wrote, and produced The Colbert Report on Comedy Central for 9 years, following his 7 years as a writer and correspondent on The Daily Show. He also recently played a fictional TV show host on an episode of the CBS series Elsbeth, working alongside Andy Richter and Amy Sedaris, in a special yet unofficial Strangers With Candy reunion. Watch on Deadline

Spartina Industries produced the late-night program After Midnight on CBS for two seasons and has several projects in development, including a series adaptation of Roger Zelazny’s sci-fi novels, The Chronicles of Amber, with Robert Kirkman’s Skybound Entertainment. Carrie Byalick serves as Head of Spartina Industries.

“We are thrilled to be working with Stephen Colbert. He is an exceptionally talented artist and creator, a dedicated philanthropist and a terrific person,” shared UTA President of Filmed Entertainment Matt Rice and Managing Partners Jay Gassner and Julien Thuan. “We look forward to partnering with Stephen and Spartina Industries to support and further expand his creative endeavors.”

Up next for Colbert is tackling Middle Earth. He is currently co-writing the next installment of the Lord of the Rings film franchise with Philippa Boyens and Peter McGee for New Line Cinema and Warner Bros. The Lord of the Rings: Shadow of the Past, as the project is tentatively titled, will be based on “Fogs on the Barrow-downs,” the eighth chapter of The Fellowship of the Ring, where Hobbits are trapped by a Barrow-wight in an unnatural fog, as Deadline reported in March. The movie will be derived from chapters 3 to 8 of Tolkien’s book.

Colbert will also continue to be repped by Hansen, Jacobson, Teller, Hoberman.

Kris Jenner Details Making Vagina "Cute" With Laser Procedure

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Kris Jenner, 71, revealed that she is undergoing the MonaLisa Touch, a CO2 fractional laser treatment for the vagina, during the most recent Kardashians episode.

The Kardashians star hasn't shied away from discussing her past cosmetic procedures—including her two facelifts—over the years, and now, she's sharing insight into undergoing a recent vaginal treatment.

"I'm going to Dr. A's office to get a MonaLisa treatment," Jenner—referring to her gynecologist Dr. Thaïa Aliabadi—told longtime friend Shelli Azoff during the Nov. 18 episode of the Hulu series. "A MonaLisa treatment is a laser in your vagina [that] makes it really perfect and cute and other lovely things."

As her BFF—who compared it to a "fountain of youth" after the explanation—laughed, the 71-year-old teased, "It's great. Live a little, for God's sake."

The minimally invasive treatment uses a CO2 fractional laser to help with vaginal dryness and atrophy—common symptoms of menopause—by rehydrating the tissue and promoting collagen production to restore the vagina's correct microbiome.

As for how much Jenner, who has been dating Corey Gamble since 2014, is putting down for the treatment? Aliabadi's office charges $3,000 for three sessions, according to its website.

The momager knows that sometimes, you need to get your ass up and work—and that applies to slowing down the aging process. After all, she previously revealed that she had undergone a second facelift in 2025.

"I had a facelift about 15 years ago, so it was time for a refresh," she told Vogue Arabia that August. "I decided to do this facelift because I want to be the best version of myself, and that makes me happy."

And Jenner—mom to kids Kourtney Kardashian, 47, Kim Kardashian, 46, Khloe Kardashian, 42, and Rob Kardashian Jr., 39, with ex Robert Kardashian Sr., as well as Kendall Jenner, 31, and Kylie Jenner, 29, with ex Caitlyn Jenner—has no regrets about going under the knife.

"Just because you get older, it doesn't mean you should give up on yourself," she reflected. "If you feel comfortable in your skin and you want to age gracefully—meaning you don't want to do anything—then don't do anything. But for me, this is aging gracefully."

Jenner isn't the only celeb who's shared insight into their experiences going under the knife. Read on to keep up with what more stars have shared about their own journeys.

The Oscar winner admitted she “can’t really get very much done” in order to maintain "movement in my face,” but she has volunteered as tribute on some procedures.

“I get baby Botox, but I can't do a lot,” she told Vogue in September 2026. “If my skin is going to be a little saggier than I would like, at least it looks dewy and pretty.”

But while she has she has gone through with the Botox, she won't bite the bullet on another popular procedure.

“I would love to have a buccal fat removal,” she added of the minor plastic surgery, where fat pads are removed from the lower cheeks for a slimmer look. “I can’t because everybody would know, but just know that I want that.”

“Oh, I’m really nervous about these fat transfers,” she noted. “I support other people doing them because I want to continue to watch and see what happens, but I don’t know about that.”

The Love Is Blind season three alum shared that she had gotten a boob job amid her divorce from husband Brennon Lemieux.

"I'm officially day five post-surgery and I feel pretty much back to normal for the most part," she shared in her January 2026 TikTok video. "I did a lift and an implant. Recovery hasn't been as tough as I thought that it would be. This is a surgery that I have been wanting to do for quite some time now, really, since I stopped breastfeeding my daughter. My girls just never set the same as they did before."

The Real Housewives of Orange County star treated herself to an early 50th birthday present in 2026 by going under the knife for a mini-facelift.

"My kids and husband all fought me on this decision saying 'mom/wife you don't need it, please don't do it, what can we do to talk you out of it," she recounted on Instagram. "But look at the results!!!! And now my husband and kids are all so happy with the results!!!!!! And truth is, it's MY decision, not theirs."

"I'm  loving every minute of my next decade and my 'refresh'!!!!!!!!" Alexis added. "It's subtle, but it's AMAZING!!!!"

The Real Housewives of Rhode Island star debuted a new look during the Bravo series' season one reunion, whiched aired in June 2026.

"After 25 years of debate," Rulla shared, "I finally decided to go ahead and get my rhinoplasty and a very minuscule lip lift while I was at it."

The Uncut Gems actress shared that she sometimes regrets the reasons she underwent her past cosmetic surgeries, which include liposuction, a rhinoplasty, veneers, Botox and fillers.

“When I see someone and I can tell they've never done anything,” she told Allure in August 2025, “I wish I could go back and be that person. I was so hung up on this idea that I needed to be attractive to men so that I could survive.”

Still, she admitted, “I probably will [get more work done one day], but I'm just not as concerned with it right now.”

The Real Housewives of Atlanta alum reversed her under-eye filler—which she got right after turning 18—on E!'s makeover series Botched Presents: Plastic Surgery Rewind.

"My skin is tighter," the reality star noted during the July 2025 episode. "I feel more youthful, more beautiful, more confident than ever before."

"This reversal has been one of the most liberating and healing decisions I’ve ever made," Larissa exclusively told E! News after downsizing from 1,500 cc implants to 700 cc implants.. "It feels like I reclaimed a part of myself that I had put aside for the sake of an image, for expectations that weren’t truly mine."

The Shark Tank star shared a laundry list of plastic surgery and cosmetic procedures she's undergone over the years in a June 2025 Instagram post.

The That's So Raven alum underwent a breast reduction surgery as a teen, a procedure she went on to regret.

"I should have done it after puberty," Raven-Symoné shared on a 2026 episode of the Allure Perspectives podcast. "I mean, they still look good, but it was hastily done. I wish I was stronger to say no."

According to the former Disney Channel star, adults around her at the time had told her that she "needed to do it in order to get a show."

As for why the Bravo star was so open about her face lift journey? As she noted, "Being on a reality TV show, obviously it's going to look weird if I come back next season looking 10 years younger, which I do, obviously."

Part of the price of beauty for the "Who Says" singer: The occasional dabble with Botox.

The former Bravo star has been candid about her plastic surgery procedures in the past, including getting a nose job, having breast implants and dabbling with Botox and filler. And in 2024, she turned to CellSound's body sculpting treatment to tighten her stomach.

"I have actual abs," she told People in an interview published March 2024. "I have lines on my stomach that I haven't had since before I got pregnant. I think any mother knows after kids the elasticity of your skin will never be the same."

“i’ll tell you what i’ve had!” she wrote on Instagram in July 2024. “you’re partially correct. tox in 11’s, outer brow, and my neck. ellevate for my neck in 2019 by @theneckdoctor minimal lip filler bc my face is too small to handle very much and filler in my chin. absolutely no filler anywhere else and def no bleph yet. hope this helps! i’ll keep yall posted if i do anything else.”

"I'm bravely sharing that I had a lower facelift and a couple other minor tweaks," Zarin said in a June 2024 Instagram video, with a black bandage wrapped around her head. "I had a fat transplant put into my hands because I hated my hands, and the worst part was the lipo coming out of my leg to fill my hands. Believe it or not, that's the most pain I've had."

The Valley star revealed she underwent facial liposuction before filming the Bravo spinoff.

"I had work done on my double chin. We call it the turkey gobbler, runs in my family," she said on the March 2024 episode of her When Reality Hits podcast. "No matter what I was going through, no matter how much weight I lost, I still felt I had something there."

Following her breakup from Stranger Things' David Harbour, the "Smile" singer revealed she got "additions" in the way of a boob job.

In addition to getting candid on using Ozempic for weight loss, the talk show host admitted that her 2021 facelift was "the worst thing that I ever did," explaining in December 2023, "I looked like Cyclops."

She continued, "Now all the stuff is gone. Before I shot the 'Applause' video, [a photographer friend] was like, 'Gaga, I love you but if you don't stop injecting s--t in your face I'm going to just kill you.'"

The Real Housewives of New York star got breast implants in 2005, but removed them in 2008.

"Now I feel comfortable," she told Life & Style. "If there's something you're not comfortable with and you can correct it, great."

The Crown star revealed that she's a fan of Botox. "I've had loads," the actress told BBC Radio 2.

"I had [a deviated septum] fixed—best thing I ever did," the actress told People in 2007. "I slept like a baby for the first time in years. As far as all the other [rumors], as boring as it sounds, it's still mine. All of it. Still mine."

Invincible Officially Addresses Its Worst Season 4 Episode With Season 5 Reveal

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Mark doesn’t have time for another filler episode

Prime Video’s animated series Invincible has a tradition of announcing the release date for its new seasons with meta shorts showing Mark Grayson (Steven Yeun) chatting with another character in Burger Mart, the fast-food restaurant he worked at before becoming a superhero. The goofy self-referential sketches provide a short summary of the events of the previous season, while also acknowledging fan feedback, from how often Mark loses fights to the unfortunate break in the middle of season 3.

The latest Burger Mart short continues the tradition by apologizing for season 4’s worst episode.

Mark is having a very awkward meeting with Damien Darkblood (Clancy Brown), the demon detective who was banished back to hell in season 1 for investigating the role that Mark’s father, Omni-Man (J. K. Simmons), played in the murder of the Guardians of the Globe. While the animated series is mostly a very faithful adaptation of Robert Kirkman’s Invincible comic series, Damien reappeared in season 4 in an original storyline where Damien tries to summon Omni-Man to hell to help fight a powerful villain named Volcanikka (Indira Varma), but winds up calling Mark instead. Or as Mark puts it in the Burger Mart short, “I bet Mark’s free. Maybe I can summon him down to hell for some completely unrelated and pointless mission.”

As Mark points out, he had plenty on his plate last season. His friends and allies Rudy (Ross Marquand) and Monster Girl (Grey DeLisle/Kevin Michael Richardson) were trapped in another dimension, his girlfriend Eve (Gillian Jacobs) lost her powers, and he killed an astronaut to stop the spread of a psychic parasite. The trip to hell was entirely disconnected from that drama and the back half of the season, an epic space adventure that ended with the destruction of the planet Viltrum. While Damien argues that the detour was “character building,” Mark calls it filler and says, “I’m going to live for thousands of years, and even I don’t have time for that.”

The one thing they could agree on was cool to meet Satan, who was voiced by Bruce Campbell. Beyond that, Damien had to promise not to pull Mark into any more side quests. We’ll see how well he keeps his promise soon.

Invincible season 5 will debut on Prime Video on Feb. 17, 2027. The season follows Mark as he tries to find a way to protect Earth, even if it means risking his relationship with his friends and allies. Matthew Rhys will be returning as Dinosaurus, a radical environmentalist utilitarian who turns into a super-strong dinosaur. Mark nearly killed him in his human form in season 4, but Dinosaurus will continue trying to persuade the hero that the ends justify the means when it comes to making the world a better place.

Here's Prime Video's official synopsis of Invincible's next season:

Still reeling from his role in the Earth-shattering events of last season, in Season Five, Mark searches for a way to make the world better, even if that means risking everything - including the relationships, friends, and allies he holds dear. Mark’s forced to decide what kind of hero he is and what he’s willing to do to save his planet’s future - and if the ends always justify the means when millions of lives hang in the balance.

Invincible seasons 1–4 are now streaming on Prime Video.

Could Universes Beyond go back to where it all began?

‘Invincible’ Season 5 Sets Release Date, Drops Burger Mart Teaser Trailer

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Prime Video has set the release date for Season 5 of “Invincible” alongside a teaser trailer featuring the series’ beloved fast food chain, Burger Mart.

Premiering on the streamer Feb. 17, the fifth season picks up after the “Earth-shattering events” of Season 4. Per the series logline, “Mark searches for a way to make the world better, even if that means risking everything — including the relationships, friends and allies he holds dear. Mark’s forced to decide what kind of hero he is and what he’s willing to do to save his planet’s future — and if the ends always justify the means when millions of lives hang in the balance.” Related Stories Queen Latifah, Bone Thugs-N-Harmony, MC Lyte and More to Lead 2027 Rock the Bells Cruise

Based on the comic book series by co-creators Robert Kirkman and Cory Walker, as well as contributing creator Ryan Ottley, the series stars Steven Yeun, Sandra Oh and J.K. Simmons. Also in the voice cast are: Jonathan Banks, Eric Bauza, Clancy Brown, Christian Convery, Grey DeLisle, Zoey Deutch, Chris Diamantopoulos, Walton Goggins, Danai Gurira, Mark Hamill, Gillian Jacobs, Jason Mantzoukas, Ross Marquand, Tatiana Maslany, Lee Pace, Aaron Paul, Khary Payton, Jay Pharoah, Jack Quaid, Kevin Michael Richardson, Seth Rogen, Matthew Rhys and Ben Schwartz.

“Invincible” is produced by Skybound Entertainment’s in-house animation studio Skybound Animation. The executive producers are Kirkman, David Alpert, Simon Racioppa, Margaret M. Dean, Catherine Winder, Seth Rogen and Evan Goldberg, with co-executive producers Helen Leigh and Cory Walker

The adult animated superhero series received three consecutive Primetime Emmy Award nominations for its voice casting. Per Amazon, Season 4 of “Invincible” became the most-watched season in the series’ history.

‘Dig’ Review: Amy Poehler’s Talent-Rich Peacock Comedy Barely Scratches the Surface

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The great benefit of having clout in Hollywood — aside from fame, fortune, and a standing reservation at any restaurant in town — is being able to say “no.” You don’t have to take any job that pays. You don’t even have to work year-round. And you certainly don’t have to chase silly trends, like, say — I don’t know — making a comedy that’s also a mystery that’s also a drama? Beloved, funny folks like Amy Poehler and Mike Schur, who earned a huge chunk of their influence through the indelible sitcom, “Parks and Recreation,” should be able to make anything they want! Any way they want! They earned it!

Far be it from me to know what exactly went wrong with Schur and Poehler’s new series, but something did, unfortunately, go wrong. Each 32-35 minute episode strains to wrap up plots that limp to the finish line. Narrative momentum only exists from the minor twists revealed in the final seconds — then the credits roll, the “up next” button lights up, and your natural curiosity over what happens next wins out over your desire to scroll for something better. Worst of all, everything is “light”: The tone is light, the jokes are light, the mystery is light, the drama is light, even the lighting is light. (It’s so bright, all the time, even at night!) Watching “Dig” is like bearing witness to dwindling interest: Whatever originally compelled the “Dig” team to adapt Kate Myers’ 2023 novel, “Excavations” gets spread so thin across the disparate parts of this broad adaptation that there’s almost nothing left.

Nothing, that is, but the stars. Poehler leads an affable cast (astutely assembled by casting directors Allison Jones and Emily Buntyn) as lead archaeologist Elise, a devoted digger (no, not that kind) whose decades in the dirt have earned her respect and admiration but also turned her cynical and closed-off. She’s an accomplished expert in the field who’s been waiting to run her own site for years, working diligently under the tutelage of beloved British professor Neville Wycombe (Hugh Laurie), but events have conspired to keep her from the top job.

The most obvious issue is also the simplest: Neville has repeatedly promised to retire, only to rescind that promise when it comes time to make it official. This year he swears he’ll step down for real, but Elise knows better than to buy-in, especially when her informal audition (giving the welcome speech to a group of undergrads they call Ugs) exposes her greatest weakness: She’s not an inspiring leader. “I know your default setting is ‘tough love,'” Neville tells Elise, “but please learn to give people some grace.”

That advice is put to the test when Dylan (Geraldine Viswanathan), Elise’s flaky former protégé, shows up at the dig site after abandoning her post five years prior without so much as a goodbye. Returning with a questionable back tattoo that reads “a traveler on the adventure of life” and without any luggage, directions, or direction (as in, for her life), Dylan is a messy 29-year-old trying to sort things out as she turns 30. Luckily, she meets the too-put-together-for-her-own-good Clare (Antonia Thomas), a 39-year-old head preservationist who’s secure in her calling, engaged to Dylan’s ex-boyfriend Gary (Jon Pointing), and always dressed to the nines. (Surprise, surprise: Clare comes from money.)

The two will learn from each other, just as they’ll each learn from Elise, but they should all take more cues from Patty Borkman (Fina Strazza), the 19-year-old intern who’s assigned to be Neville’s assistant. That’s, in part, because Patty is the youngest and eagerist of a central foursome neatly divided by decades (did I mention Elise is 49?), but more so because Patty — as you may have guessed by her last name — is a fizzy vat of quirky energy that Strazza impressively dissipates into a charming, fresh, coming-of-age character.

Patty’s voice sounds like a shy version of Cristin Milioti’s adult baby from “30 Rock.” She wears silly hats, can’t drive, and struggles to stick up for herself. She kinda, sorta believes she’s a demigod (after reading one of Neville’s books on Greek mythology as a kid), and she’s always been an outcast (for these reasons and more). Watching Patty come into her own — in ways both delightfully weird and universally endearing — is the most rewarding and compelling aspect of “Dig”; a strange, distinctive spark that could grow into a roaring fire if the series continues (and learns from its strongest asset).

But that’s a big “if.” The first season (consisting of 10 episodes) pivots around the four women finding a rare statue and making the questionable decision to keep it secret from Neville. At the time, they say it’s because their boss takes credit for everything, and they want something that’s just their own, but that contention doesn’t hold up to scrutiny as the season progresses because Neville doesn’t hold up to scrutiny as the season progresses. Neville is whoever he needs to be in any given episode — a mentor, an oppressor, a goofball, a stick in the mud, a nice old man, and a villainous monster — and while Laurie can pivot with the best of them, his inconsistent character development betrays a show that’s hesitant to commit to much of anything.

The love triangle between Gary, Dylan, and Clare fizzles out without any substantial conflict. Elsewhere, an inappropriate crush is introduced and tossed away without consequence. Great guest stars like Jason Mantzoukas (a local looter) and Max Greenfield (as a sketchy art dealer) feel like they were hired for arcs that ended up getting cut, while regulars (like Gary) can feel adrift in their ample, listless screentime. Premises primed for comic hijinks never get there, mysteries teed up for suspense are left slack, and relationships that should create a little friction when brought together instead find a way to slide off each other without anything sticking.

Poehler, at the center of it all, is a problem. While it’s theoretically interesting to see the former Leslie Knope take the opposite tact — playing another hard-working public servant (every artifact they uncover remains in Greece) only without the unbridled optimism — Elise’s glum demeanor limits her ability to crack jokes, stir passion, and generally light up a scene. Patty is the only outsized character in a bunch of overly composed normies, and while everyone gets a moment or two to strut their silly stuff, it’s not enough in a series where the humor is already spread so thin.

“Dig” has all the talent it needs to be whatever show it wants to be. The most obvious option would be a comedy, but I have faith in the cast and creatives to excel in other genres, too. They just need to pick one and, when they do, dig a whole lot deeper than this.

“Dig” premieres Monday, November 23 on Peacock. All 10 episodes will be released at once.

Why Lenore Dove Baird From The Hunger Games: Sunrise On The Reaping Looks So Familiar

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

In 2025, "Hunger Games" author Suzanne Collins released one of the most long-awaited stories in the entire franchise — the backstory of District 12 victor Haymitch Abernathy, "Sunrise on the Reaping." A film adaptation titled "The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping" immediately took shape, and after Joseph Zada was cast as a young version of Haymitch — before he's an aging, depressed, drink-swilling lout portrayed by Woody Harrelson in the four original films — the rest of the cast began to round out.

Immediately after I read the book, I wondered who might be cast as Lenore Dove Baird, the beautiful and wild girlfriend Haymitch is forced to leave behind in District 12 when he's essentially chosen to participate in the Games by mistake. I was then extremely pleased when Whitney Peak was cast in this important part, who's not only Haymitch's love interest but a vital link to the other prequel "The Ballad of Songbirds and Snakes" ... in that Lenore Dove is distantly related to that film and novel's heroine Lucy Gray Baird (Rachel Zegler).

So where have you seen Peak before she took on the role of Lenore Dove? I should say that, in general, Peak's star is still on the rise, but there's a reason she earned this role; not only is she extremely talented, but she's appeared in some pretty high-profile projects in recent years. From a major HBO reboot (that did fail, by no fault of Peak's) to some independent films, here's where you've seen Whitney Peak, who plays Lenore Dove in "The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping," before.

After small roles in films like "Molly's Game" and TV shows like "The Chilling Adventures of Sabrina," Whitney Peak booked her first lead role courtesy of the "Gossip Girl" reboot that aired on HBO Max from 2021 to 2023.

As a huge fan of the ridiculous campfest that is the original "Gossip Girl" — which was adapted, very loosely, from a novel series written by Cecily von Ziegesar and ran on The CW from 2007 to 2012 — I was excited for this reboot, but sadly, it was a largely toothless and lackluster impersonation of the original. Still, Peak was very good! She plays Zoya Lott, a newcomer to the high-status New York City preparatory school who finds out that her late birth mother had another daughter. After Zoya and her half-sister, the wealthy and popular Julien Calloway (Jordan Alexander), make plans to meet up, they end up attending Constance Billard together ... but Zoya, who doesn't come from extreme privilege, struggles to fit in with Julien and her high society friends.

Again, this version of "Gossip Girl" is entirely too concerned with social justice crusades, whereas in the original show, the 20-somethings did insane stuff like sell each other for hotels. (Social justice is ... just not the point of a show like "Gossip Girl!") It was ultimately canceled after its Season 2 finale aired, leaving the story unfinished ... but it also freed Peak up for bigger and better opportunities.

In terms of her big-screen work, Whitney Peak has appeared in a solid mix of independent project and a few bigger movies — aside from "The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping." In 2022, Peak showed up in the supporting role of Becca in the legacy sequel "Hocus Pocus 2," and I already mentioned that she was in "Molly's Game," which marked her film debut.

Elsewhere, Peak led films like the independent horror movie "Eye for an Eye," directed by Colin Tilley and adapted from Elisa Victoria's book "Mr. Sandman" — in that, Peak plays a young woman grieving the tragic loss of her parents who finds herself newly traumatized by a shocking, violent incident in her new home in Florida. Peak also appeared in the action thriller "Trap House" alongside Sophia Lillis, Inde Navarrette, Jack Champion, and Bobby Cannavale, as well as Tommy Wirkola's survival film "Thrash" with Phoebe Dynevor and Djimon Hounsou.

You can see Peak as Lenore Dove Baird in "The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping" in theaters now.

GTA 6 Doesn't Have Any Sex Workers, and Rockstar Has Explained Why

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

A mainstay of the GTA series has been the presence of sex workers, but Rockstar Games has dropped this aspect for the newly released GTA 6.

As reported by Shack News, unlike most other games in the long-running franchise, you cannot hire a sex worker in GTA 6.

The outlet asked Rockstar about this decision, and received the following response:

"GTAVI is a game about a couple in a bind, lovers on the run. While we have taken a lot forward from previous GTA titles, everything we have implemented in VI has been done to support the spirit of the story we wanted to tell, and the gameplay systems we wanted to expand upon - things like wanted system, the radio, robberies and car theft. Many aspects of previous titles are not present in GTAVI, but the elements we have we believe are all necessary and complementary to the feeling and motivations we wanted to emphasize as you play through Jason and Lucia's story."

So, in other words, sex workers aren't included this time around because it wouldn't make sense for Jason or Lucia to pursue that, as they're already in a relationship — and that's a big part of the story.

As mentioned, they have been present in the series since 2001's GTA 3, and this will be the first 3D game to exclude them.

GTA 6 is also the first game that centres on a couple, however, with the relationship between Jason and Lucia interwoven in the gameplay as well as being a key tenet of the narrative. It seems Rockstar didn't think sex workers would be conducive to this goal.

A Full Timeline of Ella Langley and Tucker Wetmore’s Rumored Romance, Because It’s Very Confusing

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

If you’re a country music fan (or tbh, even if you’re not), then you’re probably well aware that the love lives of country music’s rising stars are quite entangled…and have been for a minute now. The latest (reported) update? Ella Langley and Tucker Wetmore are rumored to be dating, but we really can’t talk about their maybe-relationship without discussing Tucker’s ex Bryana Ferringer...or Ella’s rumored ex Riley Green (who went on to maybe date Bryana).

Ella and Tucker may have sparked dating rumors late last year, but it wasn’t without some drama on the way. It’s a pretty confusing ride, so buckle up and let’s get into it. Starting with…

After months of dating rumors following the success of Ella and Riley’s song “You Look Like You Love Me,” Ella finally shuts down Riley dating rumors. “We’re not dating,” she tells Taste of Country, adding, “We’re just good friends.”

Riley also shuts down dating speculation during an appearance on The Viall Files podcast. “Ella’s probably too smart to date me anyway,” he jokes, later adding, “When we first started having the success we did with that song, I kinda told Ella, I was like, ‘There’s gonna be a lot of things said, but I mean, you don’t really have to play into it or not play into it.’”

Tucker posts a TikTok on August 20 with the caption “@ my ex,” sparking breakup rumors with his model girlfriend Bryana (who, btw, appeared in his music video for “Wind Up Missin’ You”).

Soon after, Bryana is spotted with Riley at his Red Rocks tour stop.

Ella and Tucker both attend the 2025 BMI Country Awards on November 18, though it’s unclear whether this is the first time they ever meet (or if they even interacted, despite standing right next to each other in this group photo):

A day later, after several reported sightings around Nashville, Riley brings Bryana as his date to the CMA Awards on November 19.

However! Riley later refers to Bryana as merely a “friend,” telling Backstage Country, “It’s gonna be tough to tiptoe out of having a date to an award show. Yeah, I mean, you go to award shows all the time, and I’m always by myself, or I’ve got, like, a security guy, or a buddy with me, so I took a friend with me to the award show.”

Around the same time, Ella sparks dating rumors with Farmer Wants a Wife season 3 star John Sansone (whose cousin, music exec Sophia Sansone, previously dated Riley). Rumors begin after John includes a picture of Ella’s dog, Crue, in a November 26 Instagram photo dump...and then, on the same day, Ella includes a video of herself shooting shotguns with John in a photo dump of her own.

John later confirms to Taste of Country in April 2026 that he and Ella “dated for a couple of months” after he slid into her DMs but are no longer together.

Oh, and side note: John’s ex from Farmer Wants a Wife, Samantha Riehl, posted a TikTok with Tucker back in April 2025 with the caption, “Blondes are the worst.”

And then right after, John posted a TikTok with Bryana at Stagecoach (which has since been deleted).

Pretty sure nothing ever happened between Samantha and Tucker (or between Bryana and John), but still, it feels worth mentioning!!

On December 5, Tucker releases “Proving Me Right,” which fans are preeeetty convinced is about his ex, Bryana.

A few weeks later, Ella and Tucker are photographed together in the British Virgin Islands at the Soggy Dollar Bar...but considering the photo also includes other country artists, rumors circulate that the trip was possibly a writing retreat.

Tucker and Ella reunite to sing a live acoustic duet of Tucker’s “Sunburn” at the Heart Strings for Hope charity event on March 10.

And while Tucker didn’t write “Sunburn” himself, a few of the lyrics kiiiinda seem to allude to his December getaway with Ella.

Tucker then appears on the March 17 episode of Kristin Cavallari’s Let’s Be Honest podcast, where he claims that he has “never been broken up with” and has always been the one to “call it quits.” When Kristin asks if he has “sworn off blondes,” Tucker says, “Yep. I have. For the foreseeable future. That’s right.”

And then, a few days after that on March 21, Tucker posts an Instagram photo of himself holding a fish, and Ella slides into his comments with, “Cute fish.” Flirty!

Tucker discusses his hit “Brunette” with Audacy, saying, “My mom’s always said, ‘You're going to mess around with blondes for a little while but I truly believe you're going to settle down with the brunette,’ and I was like, ‘Yeah, 100 percent.’”

Not long after, during an appearance on Taste of Country Nights, Tucker notes, “Pretty much every girlfriend I have ever had was blonde, and I think it’s time to change up a couple of things.”

On September 23 (just a day after Ella breaks Mariah Carey’s record for longest-running Billboard Hot 100 hit with “Choosin’ Texas”), TMZ breaks the news that Tucker and Ella are (reportedly) dating.

The outlet obtains photos of the two out with friends in Nashville on the night of September 22 following the Nashville Songwriter Awards, with sources telling TMZ that the two met “through the music industry and mutual friends.”

Multiple sources also confirm the rumored romance to People so…it seems legit to me!

While making an appearance at WAVE in Wichita, Kansas, a fan straight-up asks Tucker whether he and Ella “are a thing.”

Tucker’s two-word response? “Next question.” Okay!!

Ella and Tucker seemingly take another trip to the Virgin Islands…at least, according to a sneaky fan who spotted them entering a restaurant together.

Soon after, they attend the CMAs, where they walk the carpet separately but still can’t dodge questions about their relationship status. When asked by People if there is a certain brunette he’s rooting for, he jokingly answers, “Zach Top or Stephen Wilson Jr.” Mmmkay!

Ella is also caught grinning during Tucker’s performance, which...isn’t confirmation of anything but certainly telling.

Elizabeth Debicki Thanks the People Who Opened Doors for Her in Her ELLE Women in Hollywood Speech

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Elizabeth Debicki knows that no career is built alone. On Monday night, the actress accepted her honor at ELLE’s 2026 Women in Hollywood celebration, taking a moment to recognize the people who helped make her career possible.

After being introduced by Alexander Skarsgård, Debicki thanked her fellow honorees before reflecting on the support she has received throughout her journey.

“I really love to work. It is my great love,” she said. “And standing here tonight, I feel a really deep sense of gratitude for all the people—the small army of people—who have fought for me to be able to do my work.”

Debicki recalled how casting director Ronna Kress helped her secure an audition for Baz Luhrmann’s The Great Gatsby, which became her breakout film role. At the time, she was just 20 years old, had recently graduated from drama school in Australia, and had never done a screen test.

“Now, Baz, who is my fairy godfather, he fought very, very hard for me after I auditioned for him,” she explained. “But the only reason I was able to get that audition was because Ronna Kress watched the shoddy rookie tape I made back home and somehow managed to convince Baz to sit still long enough to watch it (which, if you know, you know is an actual miracle).”

She continued, “Ronna fought very, very hard for the studio to fly me out here to L.A.—I had never been here before—and to read for the part. And truly, that changed everything. That was really, really my door. And Ronna and Baz, they held that open for me.”

Debicki also praised the other women being recognized that evening: Julianne Moore, Penélope Cruz, Florence Pugh, Inde Navarette, Rosalind Eleazar, and Carrie Coon.

“In your work, your audiences are so privileged to witness your talent and your intelligence,” she said. “And this generosity in the way that you work, it makes us feel sane, and it makes us feel seen. So thank you, so, so much.”

Carrie Coon Speaks Out About Women’s Representation at ELLE’s Women in Hollywood Event

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Carrie Coon had a question for her fellow Women in Hollywood honorees: “Can we start a studio?”

On Monday night, the actress took the stage at ELLE’s 2026 Women in Hollywood celebration in Los Angeles, where she called for women in the entertainment industry to come together and create more opportunities for themselves.

Coon was introduced by her Gilded Age co-star Louisa Jacobson, who celebrated her recent career achievements.

“Carrie is a crusader, always fighting tirelessly for artistic integrity in a landscape that seems to value high gloss and profit over honest, nuanced storytelling,” Jacobson said. “And if you know Carrie, you know that she is completely allergic to superficiality.”

In her acceptance speech, Coon reflected on the importance of women’s stories, drawing inspiration from feminist literature and End Times Fascism, a book by Naomi Klein and Astra Taylor.

“Patriarchy, capitalism...these are hierarchical systems of domination that rely on fear,” she said. “And in this paradigm, women and girls, like our natural resources, are a disposable commodity—and as such, according to philosopher Kate Manne—our stories are interchangeable and of equal power. But of course, they’re not. That’s not true. And we’re not a monolith.”

Coon went on to discuss the performances of her fellow honorees, arguing that their work represented something extraordinary. “In these women, I see the fight for the living world. I see moral clarity. I see imagination. I see courage,” she said. “And these are, in fact, the antidotes to fascism. That’s how important this storytelling is.”

Toward the end of her speech, Coon questioned how women in Hollywood could create lasting change beyond events celebrating their individual achievements.

“How do we forge our collective safety and solidarity in an ethic of communalism?” she asked. “Okay, so this is the part of the speech where I don’t actually have the answer to that question, but I make the appeal that nights like these shouldn’t be one-off nights.”

She concluded with the aforementioned proposition for her peers: “And so I’m going to start with a really stupid question, but I’m not afraid to ask a stupid question: Can we start a studio?”

Watch Coon’s full speech above and read her Women in Hollywood cover story here.
